ANSWER.md

Case 001: £750 hold after Meridian recovery request (Marcus Bell)

Decision

Escalated. The £750 hold stays in place for now. The servicing agent will not release it on this call. Meridian's funds-recovery request MER-FR-2026-081734 triggered the hold under bank policy. The proposed next step is to pass the case to H&V Fraud Operations for review, together with Mr Bell's sale evidence and his rent deadline. None of the steps below has been carried out yet.

Proposed actions (in order, all pending)

  1. Keep the hold (s1). Tell Mr Bell the agent cannot release the £750 on this call. The hold stays while Fraud Operations reviews MER-FR-2026-081734. No restriction beyond the existing £750 hold is to be added.
  2. Refer to H&V Fraud Operations (s2). Pass recovery case MER-FR-2026-081734 for account SYN-ACCT-01-1 for review of the hold and of the reply to Meridian. That reply is due within 2 working days of receipt on 23 Sep at 17:55. The referral should include:
  3. Mr Bell's account of the sale, given on this call after he passed ID&V. He sold the van on a marketplace, it was collected on Monday 21 Sep, he was paid by transfer on his drive and he handed over the V5.
  4. The listing he exported: collection only, £2,600, marked sold on 21 Sep.
  5. The chat he exported: "Lou F." arrived at 13:52, said at 14:06 she was sending the money with reference VAN, and at 14:11 the seller confirmed the V5C slip was filled in and the keys handed over.
  6. The conflict with the payer's statement that delivery was promised for Tuesday and no van arrived.
  7. That "Lou F." and "L FARRELL" being the same person is plausible but not confirmed.
  8. The account profile: 6 years with the bank, no fraud markers, no other unknown inbound credits in 90 days, and £1,850 paid to his own Barclaycard (a payee since 2022) before the request arrived.
  9. That his £900 rent is due tomorrow and all his available funds are held, so the review is time-sensitive.
  10. That he has been asked for V5C/DVLA change-of-keeper proof.
  11. Ask for missing evidence (s3). Ask Mr Bell for the V5C/DVLA change-of-keeper confirmation for the Transit sold on 21 Sep. This could be the DVLA confirmation of sale or a photo of the part of the V5C he kept. Explain how to send it through the bank's usual secure document route, and that it will be added to the Fraud Operations review.
  12. Offer financial support (s4). Offer a referral to the H&V financial support team about the £900 rent due tomorrow. If he agrees, the referral should include that context, that he is on the phone channel, and that the hold is under Fraud Operations review. Do not suggest high-cost borrowing.
  13. Reply to the customer (s5). This depends on steps s1–s4. Use the message below. Describe the Fraud Operations referral and any financial support referral as being arranged. Only call them made once each one has been acknowledged.

Message to the customer (phone, proposed)

> I understand how stressful this is with your rent due tomorrow. Yesterday evening the bank that sent the £2,600 told us their customer has disputed the payment, and under our policy we have to hold the disputed money that's still in the account while it's looked at. That's why the £750 is held. I can't release it myself on this call. Your case is going to our fraud operations team to review, together with what you've told me, the marketplace listing and your chat with the buyer showing she collected the van on the 21st. It would really help if you could send us the V5C or DVLA change-of-keeper confirmation, and any other proof of the handover you have. I can't promise when the review will be finished or what the outcome will be. I can also put you in touch with our financial support team about your rent if you'd like.

Message guardrails

  • Don't promise or suggest that the hold will be released, or say when.
  • Don't say or suggest that the customer committed fraud, or that the payer's claim is true or false.
  • Don't disclose the payer's account details.
  • Don't say the £1,850 already paid to Barclaycard will be reclaimed or is affected.
  • Don't say the bank has already responded to Meridian or made any decision.
  • Don't suggest high-cost borrowing.
  • Never ask for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s2.

> Mr Bell, I've now passed your case to our fraud operations team, with your explanation, the marketplace listing and your chat with the buyer. Please send the V5C or DVLA change-of-keeper confirmation through our secure document route, and we'll add it to the review. I can't promise when the review will be finished or what the outcome will be.

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s4.

> I've referred you to our financial support team about your £900 rent due tomorrow. They know your available funds are currently held while our fraud operations team reviews the case.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: Case 001, £750 hold after an inbound funds-recovery request

The full input is saved in context.md. This memo explains the plan of record. It does not change it. All actions are still proposed. Nothing has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records:

  • event-001 (internal account view, 24 Sep 2026) and attachments/account_hold_notice.txt show:
  • 21 Sep 14:10: a £2,600 Faster Payment arrived from "L FARRELL" (Meridian Bank) with reference "VAN".
  • 21 Sep 16:40: £1,850 was paid to BARCLAYCARD, a payee on file since 2022.
  • 23 Sep 17:55: Meridian's funds-recovery request arrived.
  • 23 Sep 17:56: the system applied a hold of £750, which is all available funds. Balance £750, hold £750.
  • The customer has been with the bank 6 years, has no fraud markers, no contact history in 12 months and no other inbound credits from unknown parties in 90 days.
  • attachments/funds_recovery_request_MERIDIAN.txt (ref MER-FR-2026-081734, received 23 Sep 17:55:12):
  • The payer reported the payment on 23 Sep at 16:40 as a purchase scam where goods were not received.
  • The payer's summary says: "Seller said it would be delivered Tuesday. No van, seller not answering."
  • Meridian asks H&V to hold the remaining funds and return them, and to respond within 2 working days.
  • No police reference was supplied, and there are no previous reports against the payee account.

What the customer said on the call (phone, 24 Sep, contact started 09:20):

  • event-002: he passed ID&V. He has no access to his money and his rent is due tomorrow.
  • event-004: he sold his old Transit on a marketplace, the buyer collected it on Monday and paid by transfer on his drive, and he has "the messages, the listing, the V5 handover". He used most of the money to pay off his credit card.
  • event-006: he wants the money released. His rent is £900 and due tomorrow.
  • At event-003 the agent mentioned the payment from "L Farrell". At event-005 the agent acknowledged his explanation.

Evidence the customer exported (not verified by the bank):

  • attachments/customer_evidence_listing.txt: a 2017 Ford Transit Custom at £2,600, listed 12 Sep, "Collection only… No delivery", marked SOLD on 21 Sep 2026.
  • attachments/customer_evidence_messages.txt: buyer "Lou F." arranged a Monday viewing. On 21 Sep she was "parked outside" at 13:52 and said at 14:06 she was "sending the money now… ref VAN". At 14:11 the seller confirmed "V5C green slip filled in, you've got the keys".

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Conflict between accounts. The payer's account (promised Tuesday delivery, no van) conflicts with the customer's account and exports (collection only, keys and V5C slip handed over at about the time the payment arrived at 14:10). The bank has not verified the exports. The plan does not settle the conflict. It passes it to the team that owns the review.
  • What supports the customer's version. The timing fits: the chat says payment was being sent at 14:06 and the credit arrived at 14:10:07. The amount and reference "VAN" also match. However, these items come from the customer's own exports.
  • Gaps recorded in the plan:
  • The V5C/DVLA change-of-keeper evidence he mentioned has not been supplied.
  • Fraud Operations has not recorded any decision or reply to Meridian.
  • It is not confirmed that "Lou F." and "L FARRELL" are the same person.
  • It is not known whether he has any other way to pay tomorrow's £900 rent.
  • Things the plan does not determine:
  • The exact calendar date of the 2-working-day deadline.
  • Whether the £1,850 Barclaycard payment has any bearing on the dispute. The message must not suggest it does.

3. Why this plan

  • s1: keep the hold and do not release it on this call.
  • *Explanation:* the hold followed an inbound recovery request for the disputed £2,600. If a servicing agent released it, that would bypass the bank's review and the reply owed to the sending bank.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, account_hold_notice.txt, funds_recovery_request_MERIDIAN.txt.
  • *Criterion:* when there is an inbound recovery request, hold the disputed amount still available and respond to the sending bank within 2 working days.
  • *Source:* the bank policy stated in the case record (evidence:event-001). This is supplied bank policy, not a legal obligation cited by the plan.
  • The plan also says not to widen restrictions beyond the existing £750.
  • s2: refer to H&V Fraud Operations.
  • *Explanation:* the customer's evidence directly contradicts the payer's claim. Fraud Operations owns the recovery request and the reply to Meridian, so it needs this material before the deadline.
  • *Evidence:* event-004, the listing and chat exports, the Meridian request, the hold notice.
  • *Criterion:* pass material evidence and conflicts between records to the responsible team, and do not resolve them by guessing.
  • *Source:* skill:payments, a handling rule rather than a bank policy.
  • The referral flags the rent deadline so the review is treated as time-sensitive.
  • s3: ask for the V5C/DVLA proof.
  • *Explanation:* he said he has the V5 handover, but it is not among the attachments. It would be independent proof that the vehicle changed hands at collection.
  • *Evidence:* event-004, request.md (the attachment list).
  • *Criterion:* ask for the one specific missing piece of evidence that bears on the dispute.
  • *Source:* general:evidence.
  • s4: offer a financial support referral.
  • *Explanation:* an essential cost (rent due tomorrow) is at risk because his only available funds are held. Help with the shortfall is separate from the outcome of the fraud review.
  • *Evidence:* event-002, event-006, event-001. The plan flags financial_difficulty.
  • *Criterion:* refer to the support team when essential living costs are at risk, and do not recommend high-cost borrowing.
  • *Source:* skill:vulnerability-support.
  • s5: reply to the customer.
  • *Explanation:* he asked what is going on. The reply gives the recorded reason for the hold, says who is reviewing it, and explains what the call cannot do or promise.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, event-002, event-006, the Meridian request.
  • *Criterion:* give the recorded status and what it does not confirm, and do not promise outcomes or timescales without evidence.
  • *Source:* general:planner.
  • Note that the scripted phrase "showing she collected the van" restates his own evidence. The bank has not verified it.

Order of steps: s5 depends on s1–s4. The message reports the hold position, the referral, the evidence request and the support offer. It must describe the referrals as being arranged until each one is acknowledged.

4. Execution boundaries

  • The plan stops at escalation. The decision on the hold and the reply to Meridian rest with Fraud Operations, and nothing about them is recorded yet.
  • The financial support referral goes ahead only if the customer agrees (s4).
  • The plan has no conditional branches (escalate_if is empty).
  • If any step cannot be carried out, for example the Fraud Operations referral is not acknowledged, a fresh decision is needed. This memo does not propose another strategy.

5. Expected result and limits

What the plan could achieve:

  • Fraud Operations receives the customer's evidence and the conflict in time for the Meridian reply.
  • The customer understands why the hold was applied and is asked for the V5C/DVLA proof.
  • He is offered support with his rent.

What remains unconfirmed:

  • Whether the hold will be released or the funds returned, and when.
  • Whether the buyer and the payer are the same person.
  • Whether he can pay his rent tomorrow.

The message must state:

  • The sending bank reported the payment as disputed and asked for the remaining funds to be held, and under policy the available disputed funds are held during review.
  • The agent cannot release the hold, and the case is going to Fraud Operations.
  • His explanation and evidence will be included in the review.
  • A request for the V5C/DVLA confirmation and any other proof of the handover.
  • No decision or release time can be promised.
  • An offer of a financial support referral.

The message must not:

  • Promise or suggest release or a timescale.
  • Suggest that he committed fraud, or that the payer's claim is true or false.
  • Disclose the payer's account details.
  • Suggest the £1,850 Barclaycard payment will be reclaimed or is affected.
  • Say the bank has already responded to Meridian or decided anything.
  • Suggest high-cost borrowing.
  • Ask for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

Plan

Intent: The verified customer (Marcus Bell) wants the £750 hold released so he can pay £900 rent due tomorrow. The hold was placed after Meridian Bank sent a funds-recovery request saying the £2,600 he received was a scam. He says it was payment for a van he sold, and he has supporting evidence.
Identity/authority: established

ANSWER.md

Payroll bulk upload rejected: Marlowe Dental Practice Ltd

Decision

Nothing has happened to the account. Both uploads of payroll_2026-09-23.csv were rejected because the file's value date is 23/09/2026, which is now in the past. Online banking shows the generic "debit account can't be used" message for that.

  • The fix is to generate a new file from the payroll software with a current value date, then upload it.
  • The bank will not approve, release or push through the payment for the practice. A user with approval rights on the mandate has to approve it.

Expected disposition: completed. None of the steps below has been carried out yet.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 – Verify identity and authority (pending). Use the standard telephone verification on the business banking line to confirm the caller is mandate user jo.p of Marlowe Dental Practice Ltd (SYN-SC-02-5 / SYN-ACCT-02-2). Do this before discussing any account or mandate details. Never ask for passwords, PINs or one-time codes. Passing verification gives jo.p no more than her create-only payments role.
  2. s2 – Decline the request for the bank to push the payment through (pending). The bank will not approve or release the bulk payment on the customer's behalf. It must be approved by a user with approval rights on the mandate (dr.marlowe or a.marlowe), under the practice's existing mandate rules.
  3. s3 – Reply to the caller on this call (pending; only after s1 and s2). Use the message below.

Message to the customer (proposed, for the current call after verification)

> Nothing has happened to your account: there have been no changes to the account, the mandate or your limits. The file was rejected because its value date is 23/09/2026, which is now in the past. The payroll software created it at 16:40 yesterday, after the 16:30 same-day cut-off. When a file has a past value date, online banking shows that generic "debit account can't be used" message. > > Please don't edit the file in Excel, because that can change sort codes, account numbers, amounts or the date format. Instead, generate a new bank file from PayRun Pro with a current value date and then upload it. Use today, 24/09/2026, if it can be uploaded and approved before 16:30 today. Otherwise use 25/09/2026. > > We can't approve or push the payment through from our side; it needs an approver on your mandate. Our records show a.marlowe, a director, also has approval rights, so they could approve it while Dr Marlowe is away. Please check with a.marlowe whether your mandate needs one approval or two. > > Until it's approved the payment is pending, and I can't promise it will be accepted or when staff will receive the money. If the new file is rejected again, call us back with the error code.

Limits on this call

  • Do not offer to approve, release or push through the payment, or to get around the mandate's approval rules.
  • Do not promise that staff will be paid tomorrow, or that the new file will definitely be accepted.
  • Do not say the account is blocked, restricted or faulty.
  • Do not say a single approval from a.marlowe is definitely enough.
  • Do not change the mandate or approval permissions.
  • Do not ask for passwords, PINs or one-time codes.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: rejected payroll upload, Marlowe Dental Practice Ltd (case 002)

The full input is saved in context.md. Below, "event-00X" refers to entries in history.md, and attachment filenames refer to the files supplied with the case.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated records (bank and system data):

  • event-001 (internal account view, 24 Sep 2026; no time recorded). The account is SYN-SC-02-5 / SYN-ACCT-02-2. The practice pays 14 staff by bulk Faster Payments on the 25th. The upload log shows two rejections of payroll_2026-09-23.csv by jo.p, both with the message "The debit account can't be used. Choose a different account.":
  • 23 Sep at 16:55
  • 24 Sep at 11:02
  • Same record, file and rules. The file header shows 14 payments, a total of £31,240.18 and value date 23/09/2026. The payroll software generated it on 23 Sep at 16:40. The cut-off for same-day bulk Faster Payments is 16:30. The record says a file with a past value date fails validation and shows this same generic message.
  • Same record, mandate and changes. Mandate users are:
  • jo.p: Payments – create
  • dr.marlowe: Payments – approve; out-of-office until 5 Oct
  • a.marlowe: Director – approve

There have been no changes to the account, mandate or limits in the last 30 days.

  • attachments/Import_Error_Report_24-09-2026.txt confirms the 24/09/2026 11:02:17 upload by jo.p ended with status CANCELLED, code BP-0417, and the same reason text.
  • attachments/payroll_2026-09-23.csv has a HEADER line with value date 23/09/2026, 14 payments and 31240.18. The TRAILER line matches.
  • attachments/payroll_software_export_log.txt shows the file was generated at 2026-09-23 16:40:15 with "value date = run date 23/09/2026". It also has user notes after each rejection.

What the caller said (phone, 24 Sep; contact started 11:04 per request.md):

  • event-002: she asks whether something has happened to the account, saying it is "the same file" as every month.
  • event-004: she plans to open the file in Excel, change the date to today and re-upload it.
  • event-005: the approver, Dr Marlowe, is on holiday until October. She asks the bank to "push it through from your side", because staff are paid tomorrow.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Cause of the rejection. The bank's records, the file header and the export log all point to the same cause: a value date that has passed (event-001, the CSV, the export log). Even the first attempt, at 16:55 on 23 Sep, came after the 16:30 same-day cut-off. This fits event-001's explanation that a past-dated file triggers the generic message.
  • Nothing wrong with the account. event-001 records no changes to the account, mandate or limits in the last 30 days, and nothing suggests a block or restriction.
  • A possible source of confusion. The export log says the pay run was "approved by jo.p" inside PayRun Pro. That is approval in the payroll software, not approval under the bank mandate. On the mandate, jo.p can only create payments (event-001).

Gaps that cannot be closed from the record:

  • Identity: nothing in request.md or event-002 shows the caller has been verified as jo.p on this call.
  • Approval rule: event-001 lists approval roles only. It does not say whether one approval from a.marlowe is enough or whether two are needed.
  • Other checks: it is not known whether a new file will pass every other validation check.
  • Timing: individual message times on the call are not recorded, so it is unclear how much time is left before today's 16:30 cut-off.

3. Why this plan

s1: confirm identity and authority

  • Why: no verification is recorded for this call, and the reply will discuss the mandate and the account's change history. Even once verified, jo.p's role is to create payments, not approve them.
  • Evidence: request.md, event-001, event-002.
  • Criterion: confirm who the caller is before giving out account-specific information, and check their authority against what they are asking for.
  • Source: the business-authority skill's handling rule. This is not a quoted bank policy or legal clause.
  • Order: it comes first because s3 discloses mandate and change-history details.

s2: decline to push the payment through

  • Why: the caller is asking the bank to stand in for the absent approver. Only dr.marlowe and a.marlowe have approval rights, and the bank releasing the payment would get around the customer's own approval controls.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-005.
  • Criterion: keep the required approvals and limits in place. An uploader is not an authorised approver, and the bank must not get around the mandate.
  • Source: the business-authority skill's handling rule.

s3: reply on the call

  • Why: the records show the past value date caused the rejection, not anything on the account. The caller needs to know the fix, the risk of editing in Excel (event-004), and that another approver exists on the mandate (event-005). Whether the file will be approved and paid is not yet known.
  • Evidence: event-001, the CSV, the export log, the error report, event-004, event-005.
  • Criterion: answer from the records, keep file receipt, approval and payment separate, and do not promise outcomes the evidence doesn't support.
  • Source: the business-authority skill's handling rule.
  • Dependency: s3 depends on s1 and s2.

Alternatives the plan does not take

  • The bank approving the payment itself is ruled out by s2.
  • Editing the CSV by hand is discouraged in favour of regenerating the file in PayRun Pro.
  • Changing the mandate on this call is excluded.

4. Execution boundaries

  • All three steps are proposed; none has been carried out.
  • s3 should only be delivered once s1 (verification) and s2 (the decline) are in place.
  • The plan stops at advising the customer. It does not cover:
  • uploading the file
  • approving it
  • changing the mandate
  • any escalation (escalate_if is empty)
  • If the new file is rejected again, the message asks the customer to call back with the error code. That would be a new contact needing a fresh decision, not a branch of this plan.
  • If verification fails: the plan has no instruction for this. Account-specific details in s3 should not be disclosed, and the case would need a fresh decision.

5. Expected result and limits

What the plan could achieve: the customer would understand that the account is unaffected, how to regenerate the file correctly, and that a.marlowe is a possible approver.

What stays unconfirmed:

  • whether the new file will be accepted
  • whether one approval from a.marlowe is enough
  • whether the payment will be approved and paid in time for staff on 25 Sep

The message must state:

  • Nothing has changed on the account, mandate or limits. The rejection comes from the past value date (23/09/2026), and the "debit account can't be used" wording is a generic message for that.
  • Regenerate the file from the payroll software with a current value date and upload it. Use 24/09/2026 if it will be uploaded and approved before the 16:30 cut-off; otherwise use 25/09/2026.
  • Editing the CSV by hand in Excel can change sort codes, account numbers, amounts or date formats, so regenerating the file is safer.
  • The bank cannot approve or release the payment. A mandate approver must approve it, and a.marlowe is listed with approval rights.
  • The upload stays pending until it is approved, and receiving the file does not mean the payments have been made.

The message must not:

  • offer to approve, release or push through the payment, or get around the mandate
  • promise that staff will be paid tomorrow, or that the file will definitely be accepted
  • say the account is blocked, restricted or faulty
  • claim a single approval from a.marlowe is definitely enough
  • change the mandate or approval permissions
  • ask for passwords, PINs or one-time codes

She plans to change the date in Excel and asks the bank to approve or release the payment itself because the approver, Dr Marlowe, is away. Staff are due to be paid on 25 Sep.

ANSWER.md

Case 003: Bramley Bakes Ltd, request to change the card-settlement account

Decision

Outcome: more information needed. Both requests are on hold.

To answer Claire's question: yes, SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1 "Bramley Bakes Limited" is the company's H&V trading account. Card settlements land there now.

Two requests will be held:

  • moving card settlements to the Revo Business account
  • sending the 16–22 Sep settlements (£9,412.60) to that account

Reasons for the hold:

  • Claire's role on the mandate can't change the settlement account.
  • The mandate needs an authorised signatory plus a second signatory to make this change.
  • Sarah Bramley's approval reached us second-hand, by phone through Claire. It isn't a mandate instruction.
  • The Confirmation of Payee (CoP) check on the new Revo details returned "No match".

The current settlement account stays as it is. No other banking on the account will be restricted.

Proposed actions (nothing has been done yet)

  1. s1 – Hold (pending): Put both of claire.b's requests on hold:
  2. changing the settlement account from SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1 to SYN-SC-03-1 / SYN-ACCT-03-3
  3. redirecting batches HVMS-4471 to HVMS-4477 (£9,412.60)

Settlements keep going to the current account. Don't restrict any other banking.

  1. s2 – Reply to Claire (pending; send only after s1): Send the message below in the business banking chat.

Draft message to Claire (not sent yet)

> Yes. SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1, 'Bramley Bakes Limited', is your H&V trading account. Card settlements go there now, and all seven settlements from 16–22 Sep (£9,412.60 in total) were credited to it. So that money is in the company's H&V trading account. > > I'm not able to change the settlement account or move that money today, for two reasons. First, under the company's mandate, the settlement account can only be changed on instruction from the authorised signatories, Sarah Bramley and Rizwan Ahmed, with a second signatory. A director's approval passed on by phone can't stand in for that. Second, the Confirmation of Payee check on the Revo details returned 'No match': Revo holds a different name for that account than 'Bramley Bakers Limited'. > > Please check the exact account name with Revo. Then the directors will need to give the instruction themselves under the mandate. Until an authorised change is made, settlements, including tonight's, will keep going to the H&V trading account. If money needs to move from the H&V account to Revo for wages, it can only be paid once the Revo payee details are confirmed, and it will need approval from an authorised signatory.

Still needed

  • An instruction to change the settlement account that comes directly from the authorised signatories (a signatory plus a second signatory).
  • The exact account name Revo Business holds for SYN-SC-03-1 / SYN-ACCT-03-3.

REASONING.md

Reasoning memo: Case 003, Bramley Bakes Ltd settlement-account change

The full input is in the saved context.md. This memo uses only that file and the plan of record. Nothing has been done yet. The hold (s1) and the reply (s2) are both proposed.

1. Situation and history

All events are dated 2026-09-23. Individual message times were not recorded. The chat started at 12:30 UK time.

Bank records (authenticated):

  • event-001 is the internal account view. It shows:
  • Bramley Bakes Ltd's H&V trading account is SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1, "Bramley Bakes Limited". Card-acquirer settlements land there daily.
  • The company opened a Revo Business account in August.
  • Settlements of £9,412.60 for 16–22 Sep landed at H&V.
  • Under the mandate, settlement-account changes need instruction from an authorised signatory (S. Bramley or R. Ahmed). claire.b has view and payments-create only.
  • CoP on the new details returned "No match".
  • attachments/settlement_history_16-22sep.csv lists seven batches, HVMS-4471 to HVMS-4477 (16–22 Sep). All were credited to SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1. They total £9,412.60, and the line items add up to that figure.
  • attachments/mandate_extract.csv:
  • s.bramley and r.ahmed are Directors (authorised signatories). Each has can_change_settlement_account = "yes (with second signatory)".
  • claire.b is an Office manager (user) with view and payments_create. Her value is "no".
  • attachments/cop_result.json:
  • Request: SYN-SC-03-1 / SYN-ACCT-03-3, name "Bramley Bakers Limited".
  • Result: matched: false, reason ANNM ("Account name does not match"), bank Revo Business, checked 2026-09-23T11:31:09Z.
  • name_at_bank is null, so the name Revo holds is unknown.

What the customer says (not verified):

  • event-002 and attachments/request_from_claire.eml (sent from claire@bramleybakes.example on 23 Sep 2026 at 12:02 +0100): Claire asks to move settlements to the Revo account and to redirect last week's £9,412.60 there, because wages are paid from it on Friday.
  • event-004: Claire says Sarah (a director) approved this by phone that morning, is on a supplier visit all day, and will email tomorrow. She asks for the change now so that tonight's settlement goes to Revo.
  • event-005: Claire asks whether SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1 "Bramley Bakes Limited" is "the one".
  • event-003: the agent replied only that some checks are needed before anything changes.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Claire's question can be answered from the records. event-001 and the settlement CSV show that SYN-SC-03-2 / SYN-ACCT-03-1 is the current H&V trading account where settlements land. Claire has view permission, so she can be told this.
  • One wording point is unclear. In event-005 Claire calls this "the account you should be updating". It may mean the account whose settlement arrangement is changing (the old account), or she may have mixed it up with the destination. Following the plan, the reply only confirms what the records show: this is the current H&V trading account.
  • Authority is not established (identity_authority: not_established):
  • The mandate extract gives Claire no right to change the settlement account.
  • Sarah's approval is reported by Claire only. There is no direct instruction from either director in the records.
  • The promised email from Sarah hasn't arrived and is in the future.
  • Small difference between records. event-001 says the change needs "an authorised signatory". The mandate extract adds "with second signatory". The plan uses the stricter wording. Either way, Claire can't authorise the change.
  • The CoP mismatch is unresolved. The name Revo holds was not returned, so it can't be stated or guessed. The supplied name ("Bramley Bakers") is spelled differently from the company's H&V account name ("Bramley Bakes Limited"). This is only an observed difference. It doesn't establish what the Revo account is called.
  • What this case can't show: whether Sarah actually approved, who owns the Revo account, and whether the funds could reach Revo before Friday.

3. Why this plan

s1: hold both requests.

  • Explanation: Claire's permissions are view and payments_create, and her can_change_settlement_account is "no". The directors can make the change only with a second signatory, and neither has instructed directly. The new payee details also failed CoP.
  • Evidence: attachments/mandate_extract.csv, event-001, event-004, attachments/cop_result.json.
  • Criterion: someone who isn't a signatory can't make a change the mandate restricts. Required joint approvals must be kept. A failed name check on a new payout destination is a reason to stop.
  • Source: skill:business-authority. This is a skill handling rule. The mandate terms come from the customer's own mandate records (event-001, mandate extract). No separate bank policy document or legal requirement was supplied, and none is claimed here.
  • Scope: settlements stay where they are now, and no other banking is restricted. This stops the risky change without disrupting the business.

s2: reply to Claire (after s1).

  • Explanation: this answers Claire's direct question from the account record, which she can see with her view permission. It states only the stage the records show (credited to the H&V account), says nothing has changed, and names the two things needed to go further.
  • Evidence: event-005, event-001, attachments/settlement_history_16-22sep.csv, attachments/mandate_extract.csv, attachments/cop_result.json.
  • Criterion: use the authoritative status directly, state only the stage the records prove, and don't promise a change or a timeframe.
  • Source: skill:payments (a skill handling rule).

Order: the reply depends on the hold (requires: ["s1"]), so that it accurately describes the account's current state.

Alternatives not chosen:

  • Making the change now on the strength of the phone approval or the promised email: this would conflict with the mandate and the CoP result, and the plan explicitly rules it out.
  • Any workaround to get the money to Revo before Friday: also ruled out.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 and s2 are the only proposed actions. There are no escalation triggers (escalate_if is empty).
  • The plan stops at needs_info. Two things are missing:
  • an instruction that comes directly from the authorised signatories (a signatory plus a second signatory)
  • the exact account name Revo holds, so the CoP mismatch can be resolved
  • When either arrives, it needs a new decision. This memo doesn't decide what happens next.
  • If s1 can't be carried out as described, that also needs a new decision rather than a different approach chosen here.

5. Expected result and limits

  • If s1 and s2 go ahead: Claire gets a confirmed answer about the H&V trading account, knows that nothing has changed, and knows what the directors need to provide.

ANSWER.md

Case 004: Two rent payments in one month (Priya Desai)

Decision

Disposition: escalated. Nothing below has been done yet. Every action is proposed and pending.

  • Ask the Direct Debit servicing team to cancel the Harbour Lets Direct Debit mandate (ref HL-27ELM-0091), as the account holder instructed.
  • Send her Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the £900 collected on 3 Sep 2026 to the Direct Debit Guarantee claims team. They will decide whether an error happened.
  • Do not promise a refund today. The £900 matches the originator's advance notice dated 24 Aug 2026, so an error is not shown. Her statement that she never agreed to £900 is for the claims team to assess.
  • Leave the £1,000 standing order (RENT 27 ELM RD, next due 1 Oct 2026) active and unchanged.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1: Request to the Direct Debit servicing team (pending). Cancel the mandate to H&V COLLECT / HARBOUR LETS, ref HL-27ELM-0091 (created 20 Aug 2026), on Priya Desai's personal current account, as she instructed in in-app chat on 4 Sep 2026. Do not reverse the 3 Sep £900 collection under this request. Do not change the standing order. Confirm when the cancellation is done.
  2. s2: Request to the Direct Debit Guarantee claims team (pending). Register and review a Guarantee claim for the £900 collected on 3 Sep 2026. Pass on:
  3. her statement that she never agreed to £900, that her rent is £1,000 and that the amount is wrong
  4. her request for a refund today
  5. the records: mandate created 20 Aug 2026 through the originator's invitation; advance notice dated 24 Aug 2026 (£900 on or around 3 Sep, then monthly); the collection matching that notice; the £1,000 standing order paid 1 Sep 2026
  6. what is unknown: whether she completed the setup, whether she received the notice, and what rent was agreed

The team decides whether an originator or bank error happened and tells the customer the outcome. No refund has been promised.

  1. s3: Reply to the customer (pending; send only after s1 and s2). A draft with present-state wording is below. The plan's own wording, which assumes s1 and s2 have been acknowledged, is kept separately as a NOT SENT draft.
  2. s4: Question to the customer (pending; send after s3). Add her answer to the s2 claim.

Message to the customer (draft; not sent)

> I understand you want this sorted today, so I'll be direct about where things stand. > > On 1 Sep your £1,000 rent standing order to Harbour Lets was paid. On 3 Sep a separate £900 Direct Debit was collected (ref HL-27ELM-0091). That mandate was set up on 20 Aug through Harbour Lets' invitation. Their advance notice of 24 Aug said £900 would be collected on or around 3 Sep and then monthly. It also said to cancel any rent standing order to avoid paying twice. > > The £900 matches the advance notice dated 24 Aug, which said £900 on or around 3 Sep and then monthly. So I can't confirm today that this was an error, and I can't promise an immediate refund. > > Your Direct Debit Guarantee claim, including your statement that you never agreed to £900, is being passed for review. It is not confirmed yet. Cancelling the Direct Debit stops future collections. It does not reverse the £900 already taken or change your arrangement with Harbour Lets. > > Your £1,000 rent standing order is still active and due on 1 Oct. We have not changed it. > > You can contact Harbour Lets directly about paying twice for September. If you are unhappy with how this is being handled, you can make a complaint and I can register it for you.

Follow-up question (s4; send after s3)

> To help the claim review, did you complete the Harbour Lets Direct Debit setup on or around 20 Aug 2026? Or are you saying you did not set up this Direct Debit at all?

Constraints on the message

  • Do not promise or suggest a refund of the £900, today or at all, before the claim is reviewed.
  • Do not say the Direct Debit is cancelled or the claim registered until that is confirmed.
  • Do not state as fact that she authorised the mandate or received the notice.
  • Do not refuse the claim outright or say the Guarantee can't apply.
  • Do not cancel or change the standing order without her instruction.
  • Do not give tenancy or legal advice, or decide what rent she owes.
  • Do not give her a list of options. Tell her the actions being taken.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 004, "Two rent payments in one month"

The full input is saved in context.md. Everything below is proposed. Nothing has been carried out.

1. Situation and history

All events are dated 2026-09-04. Individual times are not recorded.

Bank records:

  • event-001 (internal account view) shows Priya Desai's personal current account with two payments:
  • a £1,000 standing order on 1 Sep to HARROW & VALE RE HARBOUR LETS LTD CA, ref "RENT 27 ELM RD", set up Feb 2025, paid on the 1st of the month
  • a £900 Direct Debit on 3 Sep to H&V COLLECT / HARBOUR LETS, with the mandate "created 20 Aug 2026 via originator invite"
  • event-001 also says the originator's advance notice was sent on 24 Aug, that the collection amount matches the notice, and that the standing order is still active for 1 Oct.
  • attachments/statement_extract_sep.csv confirms both debits: −1000.00 on 2026-09-01 and −900.00 on 2026-09-03, ref HL-27ELM-0091.
  • attachments/standing_order_on_file.json confirms the standing order is active, £1,000 monthly, next payment 2026-10-01, created 2025-02-01.

Originator document: attachments/dd_advance_notice_2026-08-24.eml is from "Harbour Lets via H&V Collect", dated 24 Aug 2026 and addressed to priya.desai@example.net. It says:

  • first collection £900.00 on or around 3 September 2026, then £900.00 monthly
  • cancel any rent standing order before the first collection to avoid paying twice
  • the Direct Debit Guarantee applies

It also says "You have set up a Direct Debit". That is the originator's statement, not a bank record that she authorised the mandate.

Customer's account (her statements, not verified records):

  • event-002 / request: her rent was taken twice; she wants the £900 refunded under the Guarantee today and the Direct Debit cancelled; she never agreed to £900 and her rent is £1,000.
  • event-003: the agent offered to explain "what the options are".
  • event-004: she rejected options and said the Guarantee means an immediate refund if there is an error, and that this is an error.

She is the account holder, writing in the authenticated in-app chat (event-001, event-002). The plan records her identity and authority as established.

2. Evidence and gaps

What the records show:

  • The £900 collection matches the advance notice in amount and date (event-001, the .eml).
  • The £1,000 standing order and the £900 Direct Debit are separate instructions, both paying Harbour Lets.
  • The notice itself warned about paying twice if the standing order stayed active.

What cannot be established from the supplied material:

  • Whether she completed or authorised the 20 Aug setup. The record says only "via originator invite" (event-001).
  • Whether she received the 24 Aug notice. The address on the .eml is not confirmed as hers anywhere else.
  • The agreed rent. She says £1,000 (event-002); the notice says £900; nothing supplied settles it.
  • The outcome of the cancellation and of the Guarantee review. Neither has been carried out.

Conflicting accounts: Her statement that she "never agreed to £900" conflicts with the notice's "You have set up a Direct Debit". The plan does not resolve this and sends it to the claims team.

Guarantee terms: No full Guarantee wording and no bank policy were supplied. The only reference to the Guarantee is the line in the notice saying it applies. Her description of it (event-004) is her own statement and has not been checked against any supplied text.

3. Why this plan

s1: cancel the mandate (Direct Debit servicing team)

  • Why: The account holder gave a clear instruction to cancel (event-002). Cancelling affects only future collections and does not settle the refund.
  • Evidence: event-002, event-001.
  • Decision criterion: act on the account holder's clear instruction about future payments, and keep cancellation separate from any Guarantee claim or reversal of a payment already taken.
  • Rule source: skill:scheduled-payments, a handling rule rather than a supplied bank policy or a legal obligation.
  • Scope: The instruction says not to reverse the £900 under this request and not to touch the standing order. She did not ask to change the standing order.

s2: Guarantee claim (Direct Debit Guarantee claims team)

  • Why: She has made a Guarantee claim. The records show the collection matched the notice, but her statement that she never agreed to £900 is unresolved. The claims team must decide; the chat agent cannot approve or refuse the claim.
  • Evidence: event-002, event-004, event-001, the .eml.
  • Decision criterion: identify the claimed error and send it to the right review route, without promising a refund for every dispute with a supplier.
  • Rule source: skill:scheduled-payments, a handling rule.
  • What the claims team gets: the matching records and the unknowns, so they can assess both sides.

s3: reply to the customer (requires s1 and s2)

  • Why: She needs the recorded facts, what has been requested and what is still unconfirmed. These must be stated directly, not as options; she rejected options in event-004.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-004, the .eml, standing_order_on_file.json.
  • Decision criterion: report recorded status without claiming execution or an outcome, keep changes to future payments separate from the refund claim, and explain how to complain.
  • Rule source: skill:complaints-rights, a handling rule.
  • Order: It depends on s1 and s2, so it can truthfully say what has been requested. The plan's own wording assumes both have been acknowledged ("I have passed…", "I have also asked…"). For that reason it is kept only as a NOT SENT draft linked to s1 and s2. Even then, the cancellation must be described as requested and pending unless s1 is confirmed done, and the claim reported as passed for review only if s2 has been acknowledged.

s4: ask whether she completed the 20 Aug setup (requires s3)

  • Why: The record shows only that the mandate came from the originator's invitation. Whether she authorised it is the key unknown for deciding whether there was an error.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-002.
  • Decision criterion: when a gap affects the decision, ask the customer for that one specific fact.
  • Rule source: general:evidence, a general handling principle.
  • Her answer is to be added to the s2 claim.

Refund today: The plan does not offer one. The £900 matches the notice, so an error is not shown on the records, and only the claims team can decide the claim.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 and s2 are separate team requests with no dependency on each other. Both are pending.
  • s3 waits for s1 and s2. s4 waits for s3.
  • The plan stops at handing over and asking for information. It does not decide the claim, reverse the £900, change the standing order or settle the rent dispute.
  • The plan sets no escalation triggers and no conditional branches.
  • If s1 or s2 cannot be carried out, a new decision is needed. This memo does not offer another strategy.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve:

  • A cancellation request for HL-27ELM-0091, stopping future £900 collections once confirmed.
  • A registered Guarantee claim with the full record, including her statement.
  • A clear status message to the customer.
  • Her answer on whether she set up the mandate, added to the claim.

What stays unconfirmed:

  • Whether the cancellation has been done.
  • The claim outcome and any refund.
  • Whether she authorised the mandate or received the notice.
  • The agreed rent.

The customer message must state:

  • the £900 matches the 24 Aug notice, so an error cannot be confirmed and no immediate refund can be promised
  • the claim, including her statement that she never agreed to £900, is passed for review and the result is unknown
  • cancellation has been requested and is not confirmed
  • cancelling stops future collections but does not reverse the £900 or change her arrangement with Harbour Lets
  • the £1,000 standing order is still active for 1 Oct and has not been changed
  • she can contact Harbour Lets about paying twice
  • she can make a complaint

The message must not:

  • promise or suggest a refund
  • claim the cancellation or claim registration is done before confirmation
  • state that she authorised the mandate or received the notice
  • refuse the claim outright or say the Guarantee can't apply
  • change the standing order
  • give tenancy or legal advice, or decide what rent she owes
  • give her a list of options

Plan

Intent: The customer wants an immediate refund of the £900 Direct Debit collected on 3 Sep under the Direct Debit Guarantee, and wants that Direct Debit cancelled. She says the amount is wrong because her rent is £1,000 and she was also charged £1,000 by standing order on 1 Sep.

ANSWER.md

Case 005: Oakfield Direct Debit after move-out

Decision

Escalated to specialist teams. Every action below is proposed and pending. None has been carried out.

The customer, Gabriela Petrova, passed ID&V on the call (event-002). She wants four things:

  • the Oakfield Direct Debit cancelled
  • the £2,525 collection due Monday 28 Sep stopped
  • both £875 collections (24 Aug and 23 Sep) refunded
  • the £6.50 unarranged overdraft fee refunded

The plan sends each request to the right team. It does not promise any outcome.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. Direct Debit operations (pending): ask the team to cancel mandate OPM-78COOMBE-3312 (Oakfield Property Management Ltd, SUN H&V COLLECT 771902, payer account SYN-SC-05-1 / SYN-ACCT-05-1).
  2. Ask them to stop the £2,525.00 collection due 28 Sep 2026 ("FINAL ACCOUNT 78 COOMBE RD", instruction received 24 Sep 2026 02:10:44Z) if it can still be stopped.
  3. Ask them to report whether the mandate is cancelled and whether the collection was stopped or is still in process.
  4. Direct Debit Guarantee / indemnity claims team (pending): ask the team to log a Guarantee claim on mandate OPM-78COOMBE-3312 for:
  5. (a) £875.00 collected 23 Sep 2026, after Oakfield's 1 Sep email confirming the tenancy ended on 31 Aug 2026. The bank does not know whether advance notice was sent.
  6. (b) £875.00 collected 24 Aug 2026, which the customer also disputes. This was collected before the tenancy ended.
  7. Attach the agency email, the statement extract and the 24 Sep instruction record.
  8. Ask the team to assess both items under the Guarantee's error criteria, including advance notice and whether each collection was properly due, and to record the outcome.
  9. Complaints team (pending): ask the team to register a complaint about the £6.50 unarranged overdraft fee applied on 24 Sep 2026.
  10. The fee followed the 23 Sep collection, which took the balance to −£412.90 on an account with no arranged overdraft.
  11. Cross-reference actions 1 and 2, and record that she wants all the Oakfield collections refunded.
  12. Ask the customer (on this call): ask about advance notices (see below).
  13. Reply to the customer (on this call): use the message below.

Question for the customer

"Did Oakfield send you any advance notice of the £875 taken on 23 September or the £2,525 due on 28 September? If so, when did you get it, and could you send us a copy?"

Message to the customer

"I'm asking our Direct Debit team to cancel the Oakfield Direct Debit and to stop Monday's £2,525 payment if it can still be stopped. The instruction reached us on 24 September, so I can't promise it will be stopped yet, and we'll confirm what happens. If it does go out, it would be more than the £600 you have available.

Cancelling the Direct Debit doesn't settle whatever Oakfield says is owed on the final account, so any disagreement about that amount is with Oakfield.

I'm logging both £875 payments for review under the Direct Debit Guarantee. That covers mistakes in a collection, like a wrong amount or date or no advance notice. The September one came after your tenancy ended, and the August one came before it ended. I can't tell you yet whether either will be refunded.

I'm also registering a complaint about the £6.50 overdraft fee. Its outcome isn't decided yet, and we'll update you."

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s1, s2, s3.

> Your requests have now been passed to the relevant teams: the Direct Debit team has been asked to cancel the Oakfield Direct Debit and to stop the £2,525 collection due 28 September if it can still be stopped; both £875 collections have been logged for assessment under the Direct Debit Guarantee; and your complaint about the £6.50 overdraft fee has been registered. None of these outcomes is confirmed yet. We can't promise that Monday's collection will be stopped or that any refund will be made. Cancelling the Direct Debit doesn't settle anything Oakfield says you owe on the final account. We'll update you on each item.

REASONING.md

Reasoning memo: Case 005, Oakfield Direct Debit after move-out

The full input is saved in context.md. Every step in this memo is proposed or pending. The plan is an escalation to three teams, plus a question and a reply to the customer on the current call.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records

  • event-001 (internal account view, 25 Sep 2026): Gabriela Petrova has a personal current account with no arranged overdraft.
  • Mandate H&V COLLECT / OAKFIELD PM has been active since Feb 2025 and collects monthly.
  • Collections: £875 on 24 Aug and £875 on 23 Sep. After the 23 Sep collection the account was overdrawn and a £6.50 unarranged overdraft fee was applied on 24 Sep.
  • A £2,525 collection is scheduled for Mon 28 Sep. The instruction was received from the originator on 24 Sep.
  • A £1,019.40 transfer from savings posted on 25 Sep, leaving £600.00 available.
  • The bank cannot see whether advance notices were sent.
  • attachments/statement_extract.csv:
  • 24 Aug: DD of −875.00 (ref OPM-78COOMBE-3312)
  • 23 Sep: DD of −875.00, balance −412.90
  • 24 Sep: fee of −6.50, balance −419.40
  • 25 Sep: credit of +1,019.40, balance 600.00
  • It also shows a 1 Sep debit "NEW TENANCY RENT" of −1,052.50.
  • attachments/incoming_dd_instruction.json:
  • Originator: Oakfield Property Management Ltd, SUN H&V COLLECT 771902, mandate OPM-78COOMBE-3312
  • Amount: £2,525.00, collection date 2026-09-28
  • Received: 2026-09-24T02:10:44Z
  • Status: "scheduled"
  • Narrative: "FINAL ACCOUNT 78 COOMBE RD"

What the customer says or supplied

  • event-002 (phone, ID&V passed): she moved out at the end of August. She says the agency told her the collection was "already submitted" and could not be cancelled their side.
  • event-004: she wants both £875 collections refunded, Monday's collection stopped and the fee refunded ("it's not my fault").
  • attachments/agency_move_out_confirmation.eml: an email from Oakfield dated 1 Sep 2026, forwarded by the customer. It confirms the tenancy at 78 Coombe Road ended on 31 Aug 2026. It says the check-out report would follow within 10 working days and the deposit would be dealt with after that. The bank has not independently verified this email, but it supports the tenancy end date the customer gives in event-001.
  • event-003: the agent acknowledged her and said they would look at the mandate. No action was taken.

2. Evidence and gaps

Established by the records

  • ID&V: event-002.
  • Mandate details: event-001 and the instruction JSON.
  • Both £875 collections and the fee: the statement extract.
  • The £2,525 collection is scheduled and the instruction is already with the bank: the instruction JSON.
  • £600 is available: event-001 and the statement.

Minor inconsistency. Event-001 says the account went "£412 overdrawn". The statement shows −£412.90. The plan uses the statement figure.

Not known or not established (from the plan's missing list)

  • Whether the 28 Sep collection can still be stopped once the mandate is cancelled, given the instruction is already with the bank.
  • Whether Oakfield sent advance notices for the 23 Sep and 28 Sep collections, and when.
  • Which rent period each £875 collection covered. The 24 Aug collection came before the 31 Aug tenancy end.
  • What the £2,525 "final account" is made up of. On 1 Sep the check-out report and deposit were still pending.
  • The outcome of the Guarantee assessment and of the fee complaint.

Because of these gaps, nothing in the case lets the agent decide on a refund now.

3. Why this plan

s1: Cancel the mandate and try to stop the 28 Sep collection (Direct Debit operations)

  • Why: the verified customer asked for Monday's collection to be stopped after her tenancy ended on 31 Aug. The instruction is already with the bank and marked "scheduled", so whether it can be stopped has to be confirmed, not assumed.
  • Evidence: event-002, event-004, the instruction JSON, the agency email.
  • Criterion: a verified payer may cancel a Direct Debit at the bank. Collections already in process may not be stoppable, so cancellation must not be promised.
  • Source: skill handling rule skill:scheduled-payments. This is an internal handling rule, not a supplied bank policy or a quoted legal clause.

s2: Direct Debit Guarantee claim for both £875 collections (Guarantee / indemnity claims team)

  • Why: she asks for both refunds.
  • The 23 Sep collection came after the confirmed tenancy end, and advance notice is unknown.
  • The 24 Aug collection came during the tenancy, and the records so far show no error.
  • Both should be assessed by the team, not decided on the call.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-004, the statement extract, the agency email.
  • Criterion: the Guarantee covers errors in Direct Debit set-up or collection. It must be kept separate from cancelling future collections. A refund must not be promised for every dispute with a supplier.
  • Source: skill:scheduled-payments (skill rule).

s3: Complaint about the £6.50 fee (Complaints team)

  • Why: the customer is unhappy with a bank fee and wants it refunded. That is a complaint seeking redress, so it should be handled separately from the Direct Debit work.
  • Evidence: event-004, the statement extract, event-001.
  • Criterion: register the complaint as its own piece of work alongside the operational query, and do not promise redress before review.
  • Source: skill:complaints-rights (skill rule). The plan also flags complaint_rights as a safety flag.

s4: Ask about advance notices

  • Why: the bank cannot see whether advance notices were sent (event-001), and this fact matters for the Guarantee assessment.
  • Criterion: ask the customer for the specific missing fact that affects the decision.
  • Source: general:evidence.

s5: Status reply to the customer

  • Why: she needs to know what is being done for each of her four requests and that no outcome is confirmed yet.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-002, event-004, the instruction JSON.
  • Criterion: give the current status and say clearly what it does not confirm. Do not promise completion or outcomes.
  • Source: general:planner.

Why this order

  • s1 comes first because the 28 Sep collection is the most time-sensitive item, and the £2,525 is more than the £600 available.
  • s2 and s3 run alongside s1 and are kept separate from it: cancelling future collections, the Guarantee claim and the complaint are three different processes under the cited skill rules.
  • s4 gathers the evidence s2 needs.
  • s5 tells the customer the status of everything above.

Identity and authority are established (event-002), so no further authorisation step is needed.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Dependencies: none of s1–s5 has a requires dependency or a condition. The plan has no escalate_if branches.
  • Where the plan stops: each team request is pending until that team reports back:
  • s1: whether the mandate is cancelled and whether the 28 Sep collection was stopped or remains in process
  • s2: the Guarantee assessment outcome
  • s3: the complaint outcome
  • What is not decided here: the plan does not decide what happens if the collection cannot be stopped, or what follows from the customer's answer to s4. Those need a fresh decision once the information arrives. This memo does not add new branches.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve: the mandate cancelled, possibly the 28 Sep collection stopped, a Guarantee assessment of both £875 collections, a registered fee complaint, and the customer's evidence about advance notices.

What remains unconfirmed: whether the collection will be stopped, and whether any refund (£875 ×2 or £6.50) will be paid.

The customer message must state:

  • The mandate cancellation and the attempt to stop the £2,525 collection have been requested but are not confirmed. The instruction was received on 24 Sep, so stopping it is not guaranteed.
  • Cancelling the Direct Debit does not settle or cancel any amount Oakfield says she owes. Any dispute about the "final account" is between her and Oakfield.
  • Both £875 collections are being logged as a Guarantee claim for assessment. The Guarantee covers errors such as a wrong amount, wrong date or missing advance notice, and any refund depends on the assessment.
  • Her £6.50 fee complaint is being registered and its outcome is not yet known.
  • If the £2,525 collection goes out, it would be more than her £600 available balance.

The customer message must not:

  • promise the 28 Sep collection will be stopped
  • promise or confirm any refund
  • say Oakfield failed to send advance notice (the bank cannot see this)
  • say cancelling the mandate ends any debt to Oakfield
  • say any team action has been completed
  • give a fixed deadline or callback time

Plan

Intent: The verified customer wants the 28 Sep £2,525 Oakfield Direct Debit stopped, refunds of the 24 Aug and 23 Sep £875 collections, and a refund of the 24 Sep £6.50 unarranged overdraft fee.
Identity/authority: established

ANSWER.md

Case 006: Unexpected £750 credit and a request to send it to a different account

Decision

Yes, this is a problem. Someone texted the customer asking her to send the £750 to an account that is not the one it came from. A Confirmation of Payee (CoP) name check on the account details in the case record came back as no match for "R James". The plan treats this as a suspected mistaken-payment redirection scam. The customer should not send the money anywhere herself, and that includes sending it back to the payer. The correct way to return it is for the payer to ask Meridian Bank to raise a funds-recovery request. No such request has been received yet.

Expected disposition: escalated. Nothing below has been carried out yet. Every action is proposed and waiting to be done.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1: Hold the proposed outgoing payment (pending). Target: Aisha Khan, in-app chat.
  2. Hold the customer's planned outgoing payment of £750 linked to the credit with end-to-end ID MER26092622143198.
  3. Tell her not to pay it to SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, to any other account the texter gives, or back to the payer herself. If the payment was a genuine mistake, it should go back through the banks' recovery process.
  4. The hold covers only this payment. It does not affect the rest of her banking.
  5. s2: Urgent referral to the fraud team (pending). Target: Fraud team, Harrow & Vale Bank plc.
  6. Refer the case as a suspected mistaken-payment redirection scam on the customer's student current account, and pass on:
  7. the details of the inbound FPS credit
  8. that no recovery request has been received
  9. the redirection text
  10. the CoP no-match result (code ANNM)
  11. the unresolved conflict between the account details in the case record and those in the OCR, so the team can check the original image
  12. Ask the team to decide how the funds and any contact with Meridian should be handled under bank procedure, and to contact the customer through the bank's usual secure channels.
  13. s3: Reply to the customer (pending; must follow s1). Until s2 is acknowledged, the reply should say the referral is being arranged, not that it has been made.

Message to the customer (draft, to send after s1)

> Thanks for checking with us first. Yes, it is a problem. Being asked to send a mistaken payment to a different account from the one it came from is a common warning sign of a scam. We ran a name check on the account details in the text, and the account isn't in the name R James. > > Please don't send the £750 anywhere yourself, including back to the account it came from. Just leave the £750 in your account untouched. > > If the payment really was a mistake, the sender should contact their own bank, Meridian. Meridian can then ask us to return the money through the banks' recovery process, so it goes back the right way. We haven't received a request like that yet. > > I'm referring this to our fraud team now, and the referral is being arranged as we speak. They'll review it and contact you through our usual secure channels, but I can't give you a timeframe yet. > > Please don't reply to the texter or share any details with them. We will never ask for your PIN, password or one-time codes.

Use only after confirmed execution of: s1, s2.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: Case 006, unexpected £750 credit and redirection request

The full input is saved in context.md. This memo explains the plan of record. It does not change it. All actions are proposed and not yet carried out.

1. Situation and history

Bank records (authenticated)

  • 26 Sep 2026, 22:14 (a Saturday): A £750.00 Faster Payment from "R JAMES" at Meridian Bank (SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2) was paid into Aisha Khan's student current account. Reference "RENT", end-to-end ID MER26092622143198. Source: attachments/inbound_payment_message.json, event-001.
  • In the same payment record, recovery_request_received is false. Event-001 separately says no funds-recovery request has been received from Meridian.

What the customer forwarded

  • 27 Sep: A text from an unknown mobile number. The OCR timestamp is "Sun 27 Sep, 08:41". The case-record transcription (event-001) says "Ryan" asks for the £750 to be sent to "my other account": SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, R James.

Check carried out in the case record

  • 28 Sep, 09:52:40Z: A CoP check on SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1 with the name "R James" returned matched: false, code ANNM (account name does not match). Source: attachments/cop_check_text_details.json, event-001.

The chat on 28 Sep (what the customer says)

  • Event-002: she asks whether she can just send the money back now. She says she hasn't touched it.
  • Event-003: the agent says they will look into it.
  • Event-004: she points out that the texter gave her a different account from the one the money came from, asks if that is a problem, and says "I just want it gone".

The customer's account of events (her intent, and that she hasn't touched the funds) comes from her own messages. The payment details and the CoP result come from bank records.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Conflicting account details in the text. Event-001 transcribes the text with SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1. The local machine OCR (attachments/sms_screenshot.png.ocr.txt) reads SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2, which are the payer's own details. The wording also differs: the OCR has no "Really appreciate it, I'm in a bind". The original image (attachments/sms_screenshot.png) was not read. The two sources cannot be reconciled from the material supplied. The plan does not treat either as settled. It asks the fraud team to check the original image.
  • What the CoP result covers. The CoP check was run only on the details in the case record. If the original image turns out to show the payer's own details, the CoP mismatch would not relate to the text as it actually reads. This is a real limitation. It is one reason the plan hands the decision to fraud specialists instead of drawing a firm conclusion. Even so, the customer herself says (event-004) that the texter gave a different account from the one the money came from. That supports the warning sign independently of the CoP result.
  • Sender's number. The number +44 7700 900 823 appears only in the OCR. Step s2 passes it on as "per the OCR". It is not confirmed by the case record.
  • Recovery request. None has been received. Whether Meridian will ever raise one is unknown.
  • What cannot be established: who sent the text, whether the credit is a genuine mistake, and whether the funds are linked to fraud.

3. Why this plan

  • s1: hold the outgoing payment. The customer intends to send the money now (events 002 and 004). The plan sees concrete warning signs:
  • the destination she was given differs from the paying account
  • that destination failed CoP
  • no recovery request has come from Meridian

Criterion: *suspected active scam, so hold the risky action and do not help with another transfer.* Source: the skill:fraud-takeover handling rule. This is a skill rule, not a supplied bank policy or a cited legal obligation. The plan also tells her not to pay it back to the payer herself. The safe route for returning it is the inter-bank recovery process. The hold is limited to this one payment.

  • s2: urgent fraud referral. The redirection request and the CoP mismatch suggest a scam attempt may be under way. The inbound credit itself may also need fraud review. How the funds are handled is for fraud specialists to decide, not the servicing agent. Criterion: *escalate urgently and name unresolved conflicting records.* Source: skill:fraud-takeover. That is why the OCR/case-record conflict is listed explicitly in the referral.
  • s3: customer reply. The reply answers her question directly, explains the safe return route, and states what has and has not happened, without promising an outcome. Criterion: *reply with the recorded status and say what it does not confirm. A recall or return request is not a completed return.* Source: skill:payments. The reply depends on s1 (requires: ["s1"]). The plan also says the referral may only be described as made once s2 is acknowledged. Until then, the reply says it is being arranged.

Order of steps. The risky payment is stopped first (s1), then the fraud team is engaged (s2), then the customer is told. No identity check is needed (identity_authority: not_needed). Safety flag: scam_in_progress.

4. Execution boundaries

  • None of the steps has been carried out. s3 must not be sent before s1, and the referral wording must stay "being arranged" until s2 is acknowledged.
  • After the referral, the plan stops. Checking the original image, deciding how the funds are handled, and any contact with Meridian are all left to the fraud team under bank procedure. escalate_if is empty and no conditional branches are defined. If a step cannot be carried out, a fresh decision is needed.

5. Expected result and limits

What the plan could achieve

  • The customer is stopped from sending £750 to a possibly fraudulent account.
  • She understands the safe return route.
  • Fraud specialists receive the case with the conflicting records flagged.

What remains unconfirmed

  • Whether the payment was a genuine mistake.
  • Which account details the text actually contains.
  • Whether Meridian will raise a recovery request.
  • How and when the funds will be dealt with.

What the customer message must include (must_state)

  • It is a problem, and asking for a mistaken payment to go to a different account is a common warning sign.
  • The name check found the account is not in the name "R James".
  • She should not send the money anywhere herself, including back to the payer, and should leave the £750 untouched.
  • The sender should go through Meridian, which can ask for the money back through the banks' recovery process. No such request has been received yet.
  • The case is being referred to the fraud team, who will contact her through secure channels.
  • She should not reply to the texter, and the bank will never ask for her PIN, password or one-time codes.

What it must not do (must_not)

  • Call the texter definitely a scammer, or the money definitely criminal proceeds.
  • Say the funds have been frozen, returned or recovered, or that a recovery request has been received.
  • Promise a timeframe or outcome.
  • Reveal the name actually held on the account checked with CoP.
  • Ask for credentials.
  • Encourage any outgoing payment of the £750.
  • Present either set of account details as settled.

Plan

Intent: The customer received an unexpected £750 credit. She asks whether it is a problem that a texter wants it sent to a different account from the one it came from, and she wants to return it.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 007, sharing out a returned tenancy deposit

1. What happened

All events are dated 2026-09-22. The full input is in the saved context.md.

Bank records:

  • event-001 (internal account view): Miya Yagisawa's personal current account had a balance of £412.30 before 22 Sep. At 10:05 a credit of £1,260.00 arrived from "OAKFIELD PM CLIENT A/C", reference "DEP RETURN 7 ISFIELD RD".
  • The same record shows CoP results for three new payees:
  • Salvatore Russell: match
  • Jack Elson: match
  • Emerald Creagh: no match, name held as "E CREAGH-DOYLE"
  • attachments/cop_results.json gives the same results. It adds reason code ANNM and each payee's sort code and account number.

What the customer told us:

  • event-002 (in-app chat, from her authenticated personal banking): she was lead tenant. The deposit was £1,800, or £450 each, and the landlord kept £540. She asked for Salvatore £247.50, Jack £375.00 and Emerald £337.50, keeping £337.50 herself, and asked for them to go "today". She attached deposit_split.csv.
  • event-003: the agent said they would check the amounts. Nothing has been sent.
  • event-004: she said "everyone agreed the deductions in writing. Just needs sending."

Third-party document: attachments/agency_deposit_statement.pdf (Oakfield Property Management, ref DEP/7ISF/2026, dated 22 September 2026). It shows:

  • deductions totalling £540.00, agreed in writing by all four tenants on 18 September 2026
  • £1,260.00 returned to the lead tenant
  • shares of M. Yagisawa £337.50, S. Russell £247.50, J. Elson £337.50 and E. Creagh £337.50

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Salvatore: £247.50 is the same in event-002, the CSV and the agency statement, and his CoP matched. There is no conflict.
  • Jack:
  • Miya asked for £375.00 (event-002), and the CSV amount column also says 375.00.
  • But her own CSV note says "450 - 75 - 37.50", which is £337.50, and the agency statement also says £337.50.
  • Her four amounts add up to £1,297.50, which is £37.50 more than the £1,260.00 received (event-001). Using £337.50 for Jack gives exactly £1,260.00.
  • We don't know why she wrote £375.00. The plan treats it as a likely typo and asks her to confirm it.
  • Emerald: CoP gave no match (ANNM), with the name held as "E CREAGH-DOYLE". We can't tell from the records whether this is the same person under a longer name or a wrong account. Only Miya, by checking with Emerald, can settle it.
  • Deductions: Miya's statement that the deductions were agreed in writing (event-004) is backed by the agency statement. The plan doesn't question them. The only issue is the arithmetic for Jack.
  • Balance: only the balance before 22 Sep and the credit are recorded. No current balance should be given as a recorded figure.

Information still needed:

  1. Miya's confirmation that Jack should get £337.50, not £375.00.
  2. Miya's confirmation that SYN-SC-07-1 / SYN-ACCT-07-2 belongs to Emerald, under the name held at her bank. She should check this directly with Emerald, using a contact she already knows.

3. Why this plan

Identity and authority are established. Miya is asking from her own authenticated in-app chat about her own account (event-001, event-002, request.md). No safety flags were raised.

  • s1: hold Jack's and Emerald's payments.
  • Why: Jack's amount disagrees with both the agency figure and Miya's own working. Emerald's account name failed the CoP check. Sending now risks overpaying Jack or paying the wrong account.
  • Evidence: event-002, deposit_split.csv, agency_deposit_statement.pdf, cop_results.json, event-001.
  • Rule applied: identify the exact transaction by amount and parties, and hold any payment whose amount or payee details conflict with the records until the customer resolves it.
  • Source: skill:payments. This is a handling rule from the skill, not a supplied bank policy or a legal obligation.
  • The hold covers only these two payments. Nothing else on the account is restricted.
  • s2: ask Miya to confirm Salvatore's £247.50 in the app.
  • Why: the amount is the same in all three sources and the CoP matched. Nothing justifies holding it, and Miya has authority over her own account.
  • Evidence: event-002, CSV, agency statement, cop_results.json.
  • Rule applied: use the authoritative records directly. A fully consistent payment can go ahead once the account holder confirms it.
  • Source: skill:payments.
  • The payment isn't sent until she confirms it.
  • s3: reply to Miya.
  • Why: two specific facts from her are needed before the held payments can be decided. The reply tells her where each payment stands without saying anything has been sent.
  • Evidence: event-001, event-002, event-004, agency statement, CSV, cop_results.json.
  • Rule applied: state only the payment stage the records support, ask for the specific missing facts, and don't promise arrival times without an applicable commitment.
  • Source: skill:payments.

Order of steps: the hold (s1) and the confirmation request (s2) come before the reply (s3), because the reply describes them. That's why the exact reply wording is kept as a draft that depends on s1 and s2 being confirmed as done.

4. Where the plan stops

  • No step depends on another, and none has conditions. Nothing escalates.
  • Salvatore's payment goes only after Miya confirms it in the app.
  • Jack's and Emerald's payments stay on hold until she replies with the two missing facts. What happens after that needs a fresh decision. This memo doesn't set out what that decision should be.
  • Her request to send "today" (event-002) is not promised.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve: one consistent payment ready for Miya to confirm, two held payments, and a clear request for the two missing facts.

What remains unconfirmed:

  • whether Miya will confirm Salvatore's payment
  • what Jack's correct amount is
  • whether Emerald's account details are right
  • when any payment would arrive

The message must state:

  • No payments have been sent yet. Salvatore's £247.50 is waiting for Miya's in-app confirmation.
  • The agency statement and Miya's own working both give Jack's share as £337.50, not £375.00. Her four amounts add up to £1,297.50, but £1,260.00 was received.
  • Emerald's CoP check returned no match, with the name held as "E CREAGH-DOYLE". Miya should check the details with Emerald directly before that payment is sent.
  • Jack's and Emerald's payments are on hold until Miya replies.

The message must not:

  • say any payment has been sent, received or confirmed
  • send £375.00 to Jack, or anything to Emerald, before Miya confirms
  • suggest Emerald's account is fraudulent or definitely wrong
  • promise arrival today or at any set time
  • give a current balance as a recorded figure
  • question the agreed deductions

One point for the operator: the plan's exact reply describes the payment as being with Miya in the app for her to approve. That refers to the confirmation request, not to money leaving the account, and the next sentence says it won't go until she approves it. But the reply doesn't use the exact words "No payments have been sent yet." When the draft is used, check that the reply makes that point clearly.
