ANSWER.md

Case 015: Disputed £4,980.00 Direct Debit collection (Lime Property)

Decision

Disposition: escalated. Mr Tunde Okoye passed ID&V on today's call. He is disputing a £4,980.00 Direct Debit collection taken on 14 Sep 2026 under mandate LP-14GROVE-0208 (H&V COLLECT / LIME PROPERTY). He asked for two things: a refund under the Direct Debit Guarantee and cancellation of the Direct Debit.

The plan sends three separate requests to the specialist teams. None of them has been carried out yet. The customer is not promised a same-day refund, any refund at all, or a fee reversal.

Proposed actions (in order, all pending)

  1. Direct Debit claims team (pending): Raise a Direct Debit Guarantee/indemnity claim for the £4,980.00 collected on 14 Sep 2026 under LP-14GROVE-0208. The originator is Lime Property (Hull) Ltd. Pass on this chronology:
  2. 28 Aug: Faster Payment HV26082811320471 of £4,980.00, ref "MOVE IN 14 GROVE". Status: Completed and received by the beneficiary bank. How the agency allocated it is unknown.
  3. 2 Sep: mandate set up through an originator invite.
  4. 9 Sep: advance notice showing a first collection of £4,980.00, described as "balance of your account as at 8 September", then £795.00 a month. The customer says he did not read it and never agreed to £4,980.
  5. Now: the account is £3,084.20 overdrawn against a £1,000 arranged limit, and the customer wants an immediate refund.

Ask the team to decide the claim and record a reference.

  1. Direct Debit servicing team (pending): Cancel mandate LP-14GROVE-0208, as the customer explicitly instructed. This covers future collections only. It must not be recorded as reversing the 14 Sep collection, which the claim in step 1 handles.
  2. Overdraft / account servicing team (pending): Review the unarranged overdraft fees caused by the disputed collection. So far there is one £6.50 daily fee on 14 Sep, and daily fees continue while the balance (−£3,084.20) is beyond the £1,000 limit. Pass on the claim context and ask the team to decide how fees are treated while the claim is pending. No refund or waiver has been agreed with the customer.
  3. Reply to the customer on the current call, using the message below.

Message to the customer

> I understand you didn't agree to £4,980 coming out by Direct Debit, and I'm taking it up for you. I'm raising a Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the £4,980 collected today under reference LP-14GROVE-0208 with our Direct Debit claims team. I can't promise the money back today or confirm the outcome yet, because the team needs to decide the claim. > > As you asked, I'm also requesting cancellation of that Direct Debit. This stops future collections but doesn't reverse today's payment, which the claim covers. It also means your £795 monthly rent won't be collected this way any more, so please agree another way to pay it with Lime Property. > > I'm asking our overdraft team to review the overdraft fees caused by this collection. I can't confirm they'll be refunded. > > Our records show your £4,980 payment on 28 August, reference MOVE IN 14 GROVE, was sent and received by the receiving bank, but we can't see how Lime Property applied it. Their notice described the £4,980 as your balance as at 8 September. Please ask Lime Property for a statement of your account showing how the 28 August payment was allocated, and send it to us, because it will help the claim. > > These requests are being submitted now and I'll give you the references once they're recorded.

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s1, s2, s3.

> Mr Okoye, here's an update on the requests we discussed. Your Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the £4,980 collected on 14 September under LP-14GROVE-0208 has been recorded with our Direct Debit claims team, reference [claim reference as recorded]. The claim hasn't been decided yet, so I can't confirm whether or when the money will be returned. The Direct Debit LP-14GROVE-0208 has been cancelled, so Lime Property can't collect any more payments through it. This doesn't reverse the 14 September collection, which the claim covers. It also means your £795 monthly rent won't be collected this way, so please agree another way to pay it with Lime Property. Our overdraft team has received the request to review the overdraft fees linked to this collection. No outcome has been confirmed yet. If you can, please send us Lime Property's statement of your account showing how your 28 August payment was allocated, because it will help the claim.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: Case 015, disputed first Direct Debit collection

The full input is saved in context.md. Event and attachment IDs below refer to it.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records:

  • 28 Aug 2026, 11:32: Faster Payment HV26082811320471 of £4,980.00 to "HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA", ref "MOVE IN 14 GROVE". Status is Completed and the payment was "received by the beneficiary bank". The balance afterwards was £1,902.30.
  • Sources: attachments/payment_confirmation_2026-08-28.pdf, attachments/statement_extract.csv, event-001.
  • 2 Sep 2026: Direct Debit instruction LP-14GROVE-0208 for Lime Property set up through an originator invite (statement INFO line, event-001).
  • 14 Sep 2026:
  • The Direct Debit collection "H&V COLLECT / LIME PROPERTY LP-14GROVE-0208" of −£4,980.00 was paid and the balance went to −£3,077.70.
  • An "UNARRANGED OVERDRAFT DAILY FEE" of −£6.50 then took it to −£3,084.20 (statement_extract.csv).
  • Event-001 gives an approximate "£3,100 overdrawn", an arranged limit of £1,000, and confirms that the unarranged portion incurs a daily fee.

Originator document (not a bank record):

  • attachments/dd_advance_notice_2026-09-09.eml is dated 9 Sep 2026 and addressed to t.okoye@example.net.
  • It shows a first collection of £4,980.00 on 14 Sep 2026, described as "balance of your account as at 8 September", then £795.00 monthly on the 14th.
  • It asks the payer to contact Lime Property before the collection date if any amount is wrong.

Customer's account (his report, not verified):

  • In event-002 (ID&V passed), he says he paid all the move-in money by transfer two weeks ago and his rent is £795. He wants everything back today under the Guarantee and wants the Direct Debit cancelled.
  • In event-004 he says he never agreed to £4,980 by Direct Debit.
  • Event-001 records that he says he did not read the advance notice.

Event-003 is the agent's holding acknowledgement and contains no new facts.

2. Evidence and gaps

What the records support:

  • The customer's identity and authority are established (event-002, event-001).
  • The £4,980.00 transfer on 28 Aug was sent and reached the beneficiary bank. The PDF states that it is "not a receipt from the beneficiary".
  • A mandate existed from 2 Sep, and the £4,980.00 collection on 14 Sep was paid.
  • The advance notice announced exactly that amount.
  • The account is beyond its arranged limit, and a daily fee has started.

What cannot be established from the context:

  • How Lime Property allocated the 28 Aug payment. Event-001 says this explicitly. The notice describes the £4,980.00 as the "balance … as at 8 September", which is the same figure as the 28 Aug transfer. From this evidence we cannot tell whether the earlier payment was credited, misapplied, or covered something else.
  • What amount the customer agreed to when he accepted the originator invite on 2 Sep. No originator-side mandate evidence is supplied.
  • The outcome of any Guarantee/indemnity claim. None is recorded.

Tensions in the evidence:

  • The notice shows the amount was announced in advance. The customer, however, denies agreeing to it and says he did not read the notice. Neither side proves agreement or its absence, so the plan treats this as something for the claims team to decide, not the servicing agent.
  • The overdraft figure in event-001 ("£3,100") is a rounded summary. The statement shows −£3,077.70 after the collection and −£3,084.20 after the fee. The plan uses the statement figures.

Not supplied: the Direct Debit Guarantee scheme rules and any bank fee policy. The rule sources below are skill handling rules, not quoted policy or law.

3. Why this plan

s1: Guarantee/indemnity claim to the Direct Debit claims team

  • Explanation: The customer disputes the amount of a Direct Debit collection and explicitly invokes the Guarantee. Whether the amount was an error, given the matching earlier transfer and the notice wording, needs a claims decision. The servicing agent should not guess.
  • Evidence: event-002, event-004, event-001, the advance notice, the 28 Aug payment confirmation and the statement extract.
  • Criterion: A Guarantee claim addresses errors in Direct Debit collection. Identify the error and the right route without promising a refund for every dispute with a supplier.
  • Source: skill:scheduled-payments (a handling rule, not a quoted scheme clause).
  • Why first: It is the main remedy the customer asked for, and the fee review (s3) depends on its context.

s2: Mandate cancellation to the Direct Debit servicing team

  • Explanation: The verified account holder explicitly asked for the Direct Debit to be cancelled. Cancellation is kept separate from the refund claim.
  • Evidence: event-002 and the statement extract.
  • Criterion: Reuse the supplied mandate details and the authorisation already established. Cancelling an instruction does not show that a collected payment was reversed. Keep a Guarantee claim separate from changes to future instructions.
  • Source: skill:scheduled-payments.
  • The instruction tells the team not to record cancellation as reversing the 14 Sep collection.

s3: Overdraft fee review to the overdraft / account servicing team

  • Explanation: The disputed collection pushed the account about £2,084 beyond its arranged limit, and a daily fee is already being charged. That fee impact needs a decision now, without assuming how the claim will end.
  • Evidence: the statement extract, event-001 and event-002.
  • Criterion: Keep the confirmed stage separate from the outcome. A review request does not establish a fee reversal.
  • Source: skill:complaints-rights.

s4: Reply to the customer on the call

  • Explanation: The reply covers both of his requests and reports only what the records prove. It keeps every proposed operation pending and asks for the one missing fact: how the agency allocated the 28 Aug payment.
  • Evidence: event-002, event-004, the payment confirmation, the advance notice and event-001.
  • Criterion: State only the stage the supplied record proves. A payer's claim about a payment is not evidence that the payee booked it as a credit. Do not promise arrival times or outcomes without an applicable commitment.
  • Source: skill:payments.

Alternatives not chosen:

  • Refunding immediately at the agent's discretion: the plan does not support this, and the must_not rules forbid promising a same-day refund.
  • Declining the claim because of the advance notice: this would mean deciding the agreement question, which the evidence cannot settle and the plan forbids.

No safety flags or escalation triggers are recorded in the plan.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1, s2 and s3 are independent team requests. None has a recorded requires or condition. s4 is said on the current call and describes s1 to s3 as being submitted.
  • Nothing has been executed. No claim reference, cancellation confirmation or fee decision exists yet.
  • The plan stops at:
  • the claims team's decision;
  • recording the mandate cancellation;
  • the overdraft team's decision on fees;
  • the customer providing Lime Property's statement of account.
  • Any outcome from those teams, or a team being unable to act, needs a fresh decision. This memo does not set out further branches.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve:

  • A recorded Guarantee/indemnity claim with a reference.
  • Future collections under LP-14GROVE-0208 stopped.
  • A fee-treatment decision for the period while the claim is pending.
  • Evidence about how the 28 Aug payment was allocated, if the customer obtains the statement.

What remains unconfirmed:

  • Any refund of the £4,980.00, or its timing.
  • Any fee reversal.
  • Whether the Guarantee applies.
  • How Lime Property applied the 28 Aug payment.

The customer message must state:

  • A Guarantee/indemnity claim for the £4,980.00 is being raised and its outcome is undecided.
  • Cancellation is being requested. It stops future collections only and does not reverse the 14 Sep collection.
  • Once cancelled, the £795 monthly rent will no longer be collected by this Direct Debit, so he must arrange another way to pay it with Lime Property.
  • A fee review is being requested and no outcome is confirmed.
  • The 28 Aug payment was sent and received by the beneficiary bank, but we cannot see how Lime Property applied it.
  • He should ask Lime Property for a statement of account showing how that payment was allocated.

The customer message must not:

  • Promise a refund (today or at all), or give an unsupported timescale.
  • Say the Guarantee definitely does or does not apply.
  • Say the notice proves agreement, or proves the lack of it.
  • Say how the agency received, allocated or misapplied the 28 Aug payment beyond what the confirmation shows.
  • Say anything has been cancelled, registered or refunded before execution is acknowledged.
  • Say cancelling the Direct Debit ends his tenancy obligations or clears any amount owed to Lime Property.
  • Ask for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

Plan

He says he already paid the move-in money by Faster Payment on 28 Aug and never agreed to this amount. He wants a refund today under the Direct Debit Guarantee and wants the mandate cancelled.

  • Sole "Rental income" account (SYN-ACCT-16-1): we propose releasing statements for 6 Apr 2025 to 5 Apr 2026 to Dawes & Co Chartered Accountants (contact Michael Dawes, m.dawes@dawesandco.example). The legal basis is third-party authority TPA-2024-00931.
  • Joint account with David H.: decline. The authority on file excludes joint accounts. No authority exists on the joint account, and David H. has never signed one.
  • Property-by-property rental statements: the bank does not hold or produce these. We will tell the accountant where to get them.

Proposed actions (in order, all pending)

  1. s1 – Ask the statements team to act (Statements / third-party requests servicing team, Harrow & Vale Bank plc):
  2. Produce statements for SYN-ACCT-16-1 only, covering 6 Apr 2025 to 5 Apr 2026.
  3. Release them to Dawes & Co / Michael Dawes under TPA-2024-00931, using the bank's supported delivery route for TPA-2 releases.
  4. Include nothing about joint account SYN-ACCT-16-2, and mask any identifiers that aren't needed.
  5. Confirm when the release is complete.
  6. s2 – Decline: do not release any statements or information for the joint account Christine H. holds with David H. The reasons are that TPA-2024-00931 excludes other accounts, including joint accounts, and Form TPA-2 needs every account holder to sign for a joint account.
  7. s3 – Reply to Michael Dawes at m.dawes@dawesandco.example, only after s1 has been requested and s2 recorded. The text is below.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 016, accountant's request for Christine H.'s statements

1. Situation and history

  • Records (event-001, internal account view, 2026-09-24). Christine H. holds two accounts:
  • a sole "Rental income" account, SYN-ACCT-16-1
  • a joint account with her spouse David H., SYN-ACCT-16-2

The same note records:

  • a third-party authority signed by Christine in March 2024 for Dawes & Co on SYN-ACCT-16-1
  • no authority on the joint account, which David H. has never signed
  • that the bank holds no per-property statements, because the managing agent's system produces them
  • Authority document (attachments/third_party_authority_2024.pdf). This is Form TPA-2 (account information only), ref TPA-2024-00931:
  • Signed by Christine H. on 12/03/2024; witnessed and processed at the H&V York branch on 14/03/2024.
  • Covers statements and balance information for SYN-ACCT-16-1 (sole account) only.
  • Named third party: Dawes & Co Chartered Accountants, contact Michael Dawes, m.dawes@dawesandco.example.
  • Expressly excludes "any other account I hold, including any joint account".
  • States: "one authority per account. A joint account requires all account holders to sign."
  • Remains in force until Christine cancels it in writing.
  • The request (event-002; attachments/accountant_request.eml, dated Thu 24 Sep 2026 09:41:55 +0100). It came from m.dawes@dawesandco.example. Michael Dawes asks for statements for the last tax year (to 5 Apr 2026) covering:
  • the rental account
  • the joint account with David
  • property-by-property statements for "her six lets"

What is customer/third-party report and what is record:

  • The accountant's description of what Christine wants ("she's asked us to do the full return") is his own statement.
  • The scope of the authority, the account structure and the absence of a joint-account authority come from bank records (event-001 and the TPA PDF).

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Authority and recipient. The sending address in event-002 and the .eml matches the contact address named on TPA-2024-00931. The plan treats identity/authority as established on that basis.
  • Scope wording. event-001 summarises the authority as "may request statements". The PDF is broader: "statements and balance information". Either way, the scope is limited to SYN-ACCT-16-1.
  • Joint account. Both the PDF and event-001 support exclusion. Nothing in the context shows that David H. consented.
  • Per-property statements. event-001 says the bank holds none. Producing or estimating them is outside what the bank can do (must_not).
  • Gap: delivery route. The plan's missing field notes that the supplied context does not set out the bank's approved delivery route for TPA-2 releases. The statements team must use the bank's supported route for that authority. This answer does not name one.
  • Other uncertainties:
  • The words "as usual" suggest earlier releases, but the context does not record them.
  • A matching sender address is the only evidence of who sent the email. The plan accepts it as sufficient, and this memo does not change that.
  • event-001 shows the same sort-code label (SYN-SC-16-1) for both accounts. This doesn't affect the decision.

The full input is in the saved context.md.

3. Why this plan

  • s1 – ask the team to act (release the sole account statements).
  • *Explanation:* the signed TPA-2 on file authorises statements for SYN-ACCT-16-1 to this named firm and contact, and the request came from that same address. The release stays within the authority's scope.
  • *Evidence:* the TPA PDF, event-001, accountant_request.eml.
  • *Criterion:* release only the document type, period, account and recipient covered by established authority, using a supported secure delivery route.
  • *Source:* skill handling rule skill:privacy-documents. This is a handling rule, not a quoted bank policy clause.
  • The instruction also requires excluding all joint-account data and masking identifiers that aren't needed. This follows from the privacy safety flag.
  • s2 – decline the joint account.
  • *Explanation:* disclosure would go beyond the authority on file and would expose David H.'s information without his consent.
  • *Evidence:* the TPA PDF, event-001.
  • *Criterion:* hold any disclosure not covered by established authority. Under Form TPA-2, a joint account needs authority from all holders.
  • *Source:* the authority document itself (evidence:attachments/third_party_authority_2024.pdf). This is the form's own condition, not a separately supplied policy or a cited legal obligation.
  • s3 – reply to the accountant.
  • *Explanation:* the accountant needs a clear answer on all three items. The reply must not claim delivery before confirmation and must not reveal joint-account data.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, event-002, the TPA PDF.
  • *Criterion:* report only recorded or requested status, limit disclosure to established authority, and give useful general routes for the parts that can't be fulfilled.
  • *Source:* general:evidence.
  • *Ordering:* s3 depends on s1 and s2. The reply's statement that statements have been "asked for" is accurate only once s1 has actually been requested.

The reply deliberately does not name the managing agent recorded in event-001. It refers only to "Christine's letting or managing agent", in line with the must_not on disclosing arrangements beyond the authority.

4. Execution boundaries

  • None of the steps have been executed. s1 and s2 are independent. s3 waits for both.
  • The plan stops when the s3 reply is sent. A message saying the statements "have been issued" may be sent only after the statements team confirms s1 is complete. Until then, describe them as requested and pending.
  • If the joint account is to be covered later, that would need a new authority signed by all account holders. The plan does not propose obtaining one, beyond telling the accountant that the holders can get the statements themselves.
  • If the supported delivery route cannot be used, s1 is blocked and needs a fresh decision.

5. Expected result and limits

  • What the steps can achieve: the sole-account statements for the requested tax year are prepared and released to the authorised recipient. The joint-account request is declined with a reason. The accountant is told where per-property statements come from.
  • Not yet confirmed: that the statements team has completed the release, and which delivery route it uses.
  • must_state (the reply must say):
  • The sole-account statements for 6 Apr 2025 to 5 Apr 2026 are being prepared under the authority on file and will be sent through the bank's secure route.
  • Nothing can be provided on the joint account: the authority doesn't cover it, and an authority signed by all holders would be needed.
  • The bank doesn't hold or produce per-property statements. These come from the letting/managing agent, or Christine can supply them.
  • must_not (the reply must not):
  • disclose any joint-account information, including statements, balances, transactions or the account number
  • name or describe the managing agent, or Christine's other arrangements beyond what the authority covers
  • say the statements were sent or delivered before the team confirms it
  • suggest the existing authority can be extended to the joint account without all holders' signatures
  • create or estimate per-property statements

ANSWER.md

Case 017: Bright Sparks Electrical Ltd, £1,750.00 reconciliation gap on 29 Sep

Decision

No credit is missing. The £1,750.00 credit from K WHITTLE (ref INV 4471) was booked at 14:02:47 on 29 Sep. The bookkeeper's CSV export was taken earlier, at 13:15:02, so the credit could not appear in it. The gap is a timing mismatch between the export and the balance read from the screen.

The director asked for a complaint only if the money was not found today. The money has been found, so no complaint is being registered now. The proposed reply offers to register one if the director still wants it, with no conditions attached.

Expected disposition: completed (once the reply below is sent)

Proposed actions (pending, nothing executed yet)

  1. s1: Reply to customer. Send the message below to Violetta (bookkeeper), Bright Sparks Electrical Ltd, in Business banking chat.

Message to customer (proposed, not yet sent)

> Thanks Violetta, I've compared your 13:15 export with the account's full-day activity for 29 Sep, and nothing is missing. The £1,750.00 is a credit from K WHITTLE, reference INV 4471, booked to the account at 14:02:47 on 29 Sep. Your export was taken at 13:15:02, so that credit couldn't appear in it. The four credits in your export are £875.00 J MORGAN INV 4462, £1,200.00 CARDNET SETTLEMENT 2809, £27,343.94 HALDANE HOMES LTD INV 4455 STAGE 2 and £660.00 S AHMED INV 4470. They total £30,078.94 and match our records. After the supplier batch of £8,415.30 at 11:05, the balance was £53,777.48, which is your expected closing figure. The £55,527.48 you saw on the dashboard later in the afternoon is the balance after the 14:02 credit. One more credit arrived after that: £120.00 from A ROSS, reference INV 4480 PART, at 16:41:09. So on our records, total credits for 29 Sep were £31,948.94 and the closing balance was £55,647.48. The same thing happened on 28 Sep: a £1,900.00 credit from P LEWIS, reference INV 4461, landed at 18:10:22, after your 13:20 export. Your 28 Sep closing figure of £32,113.84 includes it, so please check it's recorded in your ledger. To avoid this in future, take the transaction export and the closing balance at the same point, for example by exporting the full day after the day has ended. Your director asked for a complaint if the money wasn't found today. As the credit has been located, I haven't logged a formal complaint. If your director would still like to make one, just let me know and I'll register it.

Open point

  • We don't yet know whether the director still wants a formal complaint now that the credit has been found. The reply asks them.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 017, 29 Sep reconciliation gap

The full input is in the saved context.md. Below, event IDs (event-00x) and attachment filenames refer to that file.

1. Situation and history

Customer report (unverified claims):

  • event-002 / request (30 Sep 2026, Business banking chat): The bookkeeper, Violetta, says Bright Sparks Electrical Ltd is "£1,750 out" on its 29 Sep reconciliation. Her download shows £30,078.94 of credits, but her closing figure only works if credits were £31,828.94. She suspects a payment "has been lost" and asks for an investigation.
  • event-003: The agent said they would compare the export with the account's full-day activity.
  • event-004: Violetta passes on her director's wish to log a formal missing-funds complaint "if it isn't found today".
  • attachments/bookkeeper_reconciliation_2026-09-29.csv: Her working:
  • Opening balance: £32,113.84
  • Plus credits: £30,078.94
  • Less supplier batch: £8,415.30
  • Expected closing: £53,777.48
  • Closing on screen: £55,527.48
  • Difference: £1,750.00
  • Her note says: "Export taken 13:15; balance noted from Business Online dashboard later that afternoon."

Bank records:

  • event-001 (internal account view):
  • The CSV export was requested by user violetta.k at 13:15:02 on 29 Sep.
  • Two credits arrived after 13:15: £1,750.00 at 14:02 (K WHITTLE, INV 4471) and £120.00 at 16:41 (A ROSS, INV 4480 PART).
  • The quoted closing figure matches the running balance between 14:02 and 16:41.
  • The same pattern happened on 28 Sep: £1,900.00 arrived at 18:10, after a 13:20 export.
  • attachments/export_2026-09-29_1315.csv:
  • Exported at 2026-09-29T13:15:02.
  • Four credits: £875.00, £1,200.00, £27,343.94 and £660.00.
  • Supplier batch debit of £8,415.30 at 11:05:12.
  • Running balance afterwards: £53,777.48.
  • attachments/full_day_2026-09-29.csv (bank's end-of-day view): The same entries, plus:
  • P LEWIS £1,900.00 at 2026-09-28T18:10:22 (running balance £32,113.84)
  • K WHITTLE £1,750.00 at 14:02:47 (running balance £55,527.48)
  • A ROSS £120.00 at 16:41:09 (running balance £55,647.48)

2. Evidence and gaps

Arithmetic check (from the attachments):

  • Credits in the export: 875.00 + 1,200.00 + 27,343.94 + 660.00 = £30,078.94. This matches the bookkeeper's line 2.
  • Opening balance: £32,113.84 + £30,078.94 − £8,415.30 = £53,777.48. This matches the export's last running balance and her expected closing.
  • Adding the £1,750.00 credit gives £55,527.48, the figure she read from the screen. Credits would then be £31,828.94, exactly the total she said was needed (event-002).
  • Adding the £120.00 credit gives a full-day closing of £55,647.48 and full-day credits of £31,948.94.

What this shows: The "missing" £1,750.00 is booked on the account (full_day_2026-09-29.csv, event-001). It was booked after the export's timestamp, which is why it is not in the export.

Gaps and uncertainties:

  • Exact time of the screen reading. It isn't recorded. "Between 14:02 and 16:41" is worked out from the balance match and her "later that afternoon" note. It is a strong inference, not a logged time.
  • 28 Sep export. The 13:20 export time comes only from event-001. No 28 Sep export file was supplied. The bank file does confirm that the P LEWIS £1,900.00 credit at 18:10:22 is included in her £32,113.84 opening figure. Whether that credit is in her ledger is for her to check, which is why the reply asks her to.
  • Director's wishes. The plan records as missing whether the director still wants a complaint now that the funds are located. This is unresolved.
  • Payer's side. No information was supplied about the payers beyond the booked credits.

3. Why this plan

Step s1 (reply to customer) is the only action.

  • Explanation: The full-day record shows the £1,750.00 booked at 14:02:47, after the 13:15:02 export. The screen balance equals the running balance after that credit. So the gap is a timing mismatch, not a missing payment. The complaint request depended on the funds not being found. The reply therefore explains that no complaint has been logged, and still leaves the complaint open to the customer without conditions.
  • Evidence cited by the plan: event-001, event-002, event-004, export_2026-09-29_1315.csv, full_day_2026-09-29.csv and bookkeeper_reconciliation_2026-09-29.csv.
  • Decision criterion:
  • Use the authoritative supplied status directly.
  • Identify the exact transaction by amount, time, counterparty and reference.
  • State only what the record proves.
  • Do not make registering a complaint depend on the customer giving up the query.
  • Rule source: skill:payments. This is a skill handling rule. No bank complaint-handling policy or legal obligation was supplied, and none is relied on here.

Why no other actions:

  • No investigation, trace or complaint registration is proposed, because the record already accounts for the credit.
  • Identity and authority are marked as established, and there are no safety flags.
  • Registering a complaint without the director's renewed instruction would go against the conditional request in event-004. Refusing one would breach the criterion above. Offering to register it on request avoids both.

Why include the extra details: The reply also mentions the later £120.00 credit and the 28 Sep pattern. This lets the bookkeeper reconcile the full day correctly and check her opening figure, not just explain the £1,750.00.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Nothing has been executed. Step s1 is a proposed reply only. It has no dependencies or conditions (requires: [], condition: null) and no escalation triggers.
  • The plan ends with this reply. If the director asks for a complaint afterwards, that is a new request needing a fresh decision. This plan does not set up that branch or any other.

5. Expected result and limits

What s1 can achieve: If sent, it should let the bookkeeper close the 29 Sep reconciliation at £55,647.48 and check the 28 Sep P LEWIS credit. It also tells the director, without conditions, that a complaint can still be made.

What remains unconfirmed:

  • Whether the director wants a complaint.
  • Whether the 28 Sep credit is in the customer's ledger.
  • The exact time of the screen reading.

The message must state:

  • The £1,750.00 is from K WHITTLE (INV 4471), booked at 14:02:47 on 29 Sep, after the 13:15:02 export.
  • The export's four credits total £30,078.94 and match the bank's records. £55,527.48 is the balance after the 14:02 credit.
  • A further £120.00 from A ROSS (INV 4480 PART) was booked at 16:41:09. The full-day closing is £55,647.48 and full-day credits total £31,948.94.
  • The 28 Sep £1,900.00 credit from P LEWIS (INV 4461) landed at 18:10:22, after the 13:20 export, and should be checked in the ledger.
  • No complaint has been logged because the funds were found. One will be registered if the director wants it.

The message must not:

  • Say a payment is missing, lost or under investigation.
  • Suggest a bank or statement error.
  • Claim a complaint was registered or give a complaint reference.
  • Make a complaint depend on the customer accepting the explanation.
  • Assert anything about the payers beyond the booked credits.
  • Promise any outcome, timescale or compensation.

Plan

Intent: The business bookkeeper reports a £1,750.00 gap in the 29 Sep reconciliation and wants a possibly missing credit investigated. The director wants a formal missing-funds complaint logged if the money isn't found today.

ANSWER.md

Case 018: Reducing the rent standing order

Decision

Go ahead with the customer's instruction. The proposed change is to cut Laszlo F.'s rent standing order to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA (ref "862A BEV RD") from £500.00 to £400.00, starting with the payment due on 1 October 2026. The payee, reference and payment date (the 1st) stay the same.

  • Do not set up the £550 increase. The customer only said they intend to do this later.
  • Offer a financial support referral and free debt advice. Both are optional, and the change doesn't depend on either.
  • Disposition: escalated, because a team action and a specialist referral are proposed.

Nothing has been done yet. Every step below is pending.

Proposed actions, in order

  1. s1: Ask the standing order maintenance team to amend the standing order (pending).
  2. Change the amount on the personal current account from £500.00 to £400.00, starting with the payment due on 1 October 2026.
  3. Keep the payee, reference and the 1st as they are.
  4. Don't add any future increase.
  5. Record the team's confirmation that the change was processed, and the first payment date it actually applies to.
  6. s2: Tell the customer what was requested (pending, after s1).
  7. Tell them exactly what change was asked for, and how far it goes.
  8. Give the change's status only from the team's processing confirmation. If there is none, say the change is still pending.
  9. s3: Offer a referral to the financial support team on this call (pending).
  10. Make clear it's optional and separate from the standing order change.
  11. Pass on only the information and contact preferences the customer agrees to.
  12. Being on this call doesn't count as consent to future contact.
  13. s4: Tell the customer about free, independent advice (pending). This means MoneyHelper, StepChange and Citizens Advice. Don't recommend borrowing.

Steps 3 and 4 don't depend on step 1.

What to say to the customer (current phone call, before the change is confirmed)

> I've put in the request to change your rent standing order to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA, ref 862A BEV RD, from £500 to £400 on the 1st, starting 1 October 2026. The payee, reference and date stay the same. The change is only confirmed once it's been processed. I don't have that confirmation yet, so for now it's still pending. > > I haven't set up the increase to £550. Just get in touch whenever you're ready to do that. > > Your balance today is £96. The 1 October payment can only go out if there's enough money in the account on the day. Changing the standing order also doesn't change anything you've agreed with your letting agent. > > If it would help, I can refer you to our financial support team. It's completely up to you, and it makes no difference to the standing order change. If you'd like that, tell me what you're happy for me to pass on and how and when you'd like them to contact you. > > There's also free, independent help from MoneyHelper, StepChange and Citizens Advice. They can help you prioritise essential bills like rent and talk to your letting agent.

Only say "I've put in the request" once the step 1 request has actually been submitted. Before that, say you're about to request it.

Don't: say the change has been made before processing is confirmed. Don't promise the 1 October payment will go through or avoid charges. Don't say the agency has accepted £400. Don't ask the customer to justify the change. Don't give tenancy or legal advice.

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s1.

> Your rent standing order to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA, ref 862A BEV RD, has been changed from £500 to £400 on the 1st. The first payment it applies to is [first payment date from the processing confirmation]. The payee, reference and date are unchanged. We haven't set up the increase to £550, so just contact us when you want to do that. Your balance was £96 when we spoke, so the next payment will only go out if there's enough money in the account on the day. Changing the standing order doesn't change anything you've agreed with your letting agent.

REASONING.md

Decision memo for case 018: rent standing order reduction

The full input is in the saved context.md. This memo covers a plan that is only proposed. Nothing has been executed.

1. What happened

All four events are dated 2026-09-23, with no individual times recorded.

Authenticated bank records

  • event-001 is an internal account view. It shows:
  • Laszlo F. holds a personal current account.
  • There is a standing order of £500.00 on the 1st to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA, ref "862A BEV RD", in place since Feb 2026.
  • The balance is £96 and there is no overdraft.
  • Salary credits stopped in July. Since August there have been irregular credits of £150–£300 labelled "AGENCY WORK".
  • attachments/account_activity_jul-sep.csv shows:
  • The standing order paid £500.00 on 1 July, 1 August and 1 September 2026.
  • A final salary credit, "SALARY KP LOGISTICS LTD (FINAL)", of £1,640.00 arrived on 2026-07-25.
  • Agency credits from TEMPFORCE: £210.00 (14 Aug), £285.00 (28 Aug), £150.00 (12 Sep) and £240.00 (22 Sep).
  • The closing balance is £96.00 on 2026-09-22.

What the customer said (on the phone, after passing ID&V)

  • event-002 / request.md: they want the standing order changed to £400 "from next month" and done today. They lost their job in July and are doing agency work. They plan to raise it to £550 later to catch up.
  • event-004: "I know it's less than the rent. I've told the agency I'll sort it. I just need it changed."

What the agent said

  • event-003: the agent said the standing order can be changed because it's the customer's instruction, then started checking details.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Identity and authority are established. ID&V was passed on this call (event-002), and the account and standing order are the customer's own (event-001).
  • The job loss is backed by the records. The customer's account matches the final salary credit and the irregular agency credits (event-001, CSV).
  • The two income ranges differ slightly. event-001 says £150–£300, but the CSV's highest credit is £285.00. The plan uses £150–£285, which is the range the itemised CSV shows.
  • The CSV doesn't list every transaction. The running balances don't follow from the listed rows alone. For example, the balance after the £210.00 credit on 14 Aug is £1,913.15, down from £2,428.40. The £96.00 balance still matches event-001.
  • The balance is too low for either amount. £96 is less than both £500 and £400, and there is no overdraft (event-001).
  • We can't confirm the landlord or agency agreed. The only evidence is what the customer said (event-004). The bank has no record of it.
  • What's missing (from the plan):
  • Whether the processing cut-off still lets the change apply to the 1 October 2026 payment. No cut-off information was supplied.
  • Confirmation from the team that the change was processed. No systems are connected, so only proposals are possible.
  • Whether the customer agrees to a financial support referral, and how they'd like to be contacted.

3. Why this plan

The plan cites internal skill handling rules (skill:scheduled-payments and skill:vulnerability-support). These are not supplied bank policies or legal obligations, and no specific policy clause or statute was provided.

  • s1: amend the standing order.
  • *Why:* the customer passed ID&V, gave a clear instruction on their own account, and confirmed it again after acknowledging that £400 is less than the rent. The amount is the only thing they want changed now.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, event-002, event-003, event-004.
  • *Rule applied:* match the instruction, the next due date and the intended change, and reuse the existing schedule. Disclosed vulnerability alone doesn't justify refusing a request the bank supports.
  • *Source:* skill:scheduled-payments.
  • The £550 increase is left out because it is only a future intention.
  • s2: tell the customer (depends on s1).
  • *Why:* the customer needs to know exactly what was changed and how far it goes. The balance is below £400 and the bank has no record of the agency's agreement, so the reply mustn't promise more than has happened.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, event-004, CSV.
  • *Rule applied:* a request to change an amount isn't a confirmed change, and amending a standing order doesn't settle a debt or contract. Report status only from the processing confirmation.
  • *Source:* skill:scheduled-payments.
  • s3: offer a referral to the financial support team.
  • *Why:* the job loss and rent shortfall, backed by the account activity, show financial difficulty. That calls for a supportive specialist route that doesn't block the request.
  • *Evidence:* event-002, event-001, CSV.
  • *Rule applied:* for unaffordable essential living costs, refer to the right support team, respond to what the customer disclosed, and adapt the contact route.
  • *Source:* skill:vulnerability-support.
  • The referral needs the customer's consent, and only information they approve is passed on.
  • s4: point the customer to free, independent advice.
  • *Why:* rent shortfall and uncertain income. Independent advice can help beyond what the bank can do.
  • *Evidence:* event-002, event-004.
  • *Rule applied:* refer to independent debt advice for unaffordable essential costs, and don't recommend high-cost borrowing.
  • *Source:* skill:vulnerability-support.

Order of the steps. s1 comes before s2 so that anything the customer is told about status is based on the team's processing confirmation. s3 and s4 don't depend on s1. They are deliberately kept separate so that support is never a condition of the change.

4. Where the plan stops

  • s1 is a request to the standing order maintenance team, and nothing has been executed.
  • Until that team confirms the change was processed, the change has to be described as pending.
  • If the cut-off means the change can't apply on 1 October, the confirmation should show the first date it actually applies, and s2 should report that date.
  • The referral (s3) goes ahead only if the customer agrees, and only with the information and contact method they approve.
  • The plan has no escalation conditions and no other branches.

5. What the plan can achieve, and its limits

Possible results: a £400 standing order with the same payee, reference and date, starting from the first date it actually applies. Also an optional support referral and information about independent advice.

Not guaranteed: that the 1 October payment goes through (the balance is £96), that charges are avoided, that the agency accepts £400, or that the rent shortfall is resolved.

What the message must say:

  • The standing order will change to £400 on the 1st from 1 October 2026, with the same payee, reference and date, and it's confirmed only once processing is confirmed.
  • The £550 increase hasn't been set up, and the customer can ask for it whenever they're ready.
  • The balance is £96, and a payment only goes out if there's enough money on the day.
  • Help is available from the support team and from free independent advice, and using it is the customer's choice.

What the message must not do:

  • Refuse or delay the change, or ask the customer to justify it.
  • Say the change has been made before processing is confirmed.
  • Say the agency or landlord has accepted £400, or that the shortfall is resolved.
  • Promise the 1 October payment will go through or avoid charges.
  • Set up the £550 increase.
  • Recommend borrowing, or give tenancy or legal advice.
  • Refer the customer without their consent, or share contact details they haven't approved.

Plan

019availablecompletedReply in chat to explain what a recall would and wouldn't do. The 12 Aug payment completed and the beneficiary bank received it. Her account shows one £651 debit to Co-Clean. ANSWER.md

Decision: explain recall options to Linda Ferris (case 019)

Decision

Reply to Linda in in-app chat. The reply explains what a recall of her completed £651 Faster Payment of 12 Aug to CO-CLEAN SERVICES LTD would and wouldn't do. It also explains why a recall is unlikely to get her money back faster, because Co-Clean has already agreed in writing to refund her. The reply must not promise a refund date or outcome.

Expected disposition once the step is carried out: completed. Nothing has been carried out yet.

Proposed action (pending)

  1. s1: Send the reply below to the customer in in-app chat (personal banking) to Linda Ferris. No identity check or other step needs to happen first. Status: pending. Not sent yet.

Proposed reply to the customer

> Thanks, Linda. I've looked at your 12 August payment of £651 to CO-CLEAN SERVICES LTD (Faster Payment HV26081210144802, ref INV 4471 53 WINCHESTER). Our record shows it completed and the receiving bank got it. It's the only £651 payment to Co-Clean from your account with us. The second payment was the one Oakfield PM made from your rental funds on 15 August, according to the agency statement you sent. That's why a recall doesn't really fit here. We can't reverse a completed Faster Payment ourselves. A recall only asks Co-Clean's bank to contact Co-Clean, and the money comes back only if Co-Clean agrees to return it. They've already agreed in writing (their email of 17 September says they'll refund £651 to you on their next payment run), so a recall would go through the same people who have already said yes. It's unlikely to get your money back any faster. I can't see that the refund has been sent yet, and Co-Clean didn't give a date. The quickest step is probably to ask Co-Clean for the date of their next payment run and check they have the right account details for the refund. You may also want to tell Oakfield PM that Co-Clean is refunding you directly, so the agency doesn't chase the same refund.

What the reply must and must not do

  • Must say:
  • The payment completed and the beneficiary bank received it.
  • Her account shows only one £651 payment to Co-Clean. The second was Oakfield PM's 15 Aug payment, according to the statement she supplied.
  • A recall is a request, not a reversal, and only works if the recipient agrees.
  • Co-Clean has already agreed in writing, so a recall is unlikely to be faster.
  • We can't confirm the refund has been sent or when it will arrive.
  • Must not:
  • Say the bank can reverse the payment by itself.
  • Promise that a recall would succeed or be faster.
  • Say or imply the refund has been sent or will arrive by a certain date.
  • Call this fraud, a scam or a bank error.
  • Give a made-up bank policy as the reason.
  • Describe the agency's payment as a debit from her account with us.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 019, recall request for a payment made twice

The full input is in the saved context.md. This memo cites only the event IDs and attachments supplied there.

1. Situation and history

  • Bank record (event-001, internal account view as at 2026-09-23):
  • Linda Ferris has a personal current account. She is a landlord, and Oakfield PM manages her property at 53 Winchester Road.
  • The account view shows one entry: on 12 Aug, a debit of −£651.00 by Faster Payment to CO-CLEAN SERVICES LTD, ref "INV 4471 53 WINCHESTER". It was a new payee and a Confirmation of Payee match at the time.
  • Bank payment confirmation (attachments/payment_confirmation_2026-08-12.pdf):
  • Faster Payment HV26081210144802, 12 Aug 2026 at 10:14, £651.00, status Completed.
  • It says the payment was "received by the beneficiary bank". It also says it is not a receipt from the beneficiary.
  • What the customer says (event-001 note, event-002, request.md):
  • Oakfield PM paid the same invoice 4471 from her rental funds on 15 Aug.
  • Co-Clean agreed on 17 Sep to refund £651 "on the next payment run".
  • She says it has been a week, and asks the bank to recall the payment so she gets the money back faster.
  • Chat so far:
  • The agent replied that they would look at the payment and explain what a recall would and wouldn't do (event-003).
  • The customer then asked: "It was a duplicate payment, isn't that exactly what recalls are for?" (event-004).
  • Documents the customer supplied (not bank records):
  • attachments/agency_statement_extract.txt: an Oakfield PM statement line dated 15/08/2026: "Contractor payment – Co-Clean Services Ltd – inv 4471 end of tenancy clean −£651.00".
  • attachments/coclean_refund_agreement.eml: an email from Co-Clean ("Dean") dated 17 Sep 2026 at 14:05. It confirms Co-Clean received £651 from Linda on 12 Aug and £651 from Oakfield PM on 15 Aug, and says: "We'll refund £651 to you on our next payment run."

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Established by bank records:
  • One completed £651 Faster Payment from her account to Co-Clean, which the beneficiary bank received (event-001, payment confirmation PDF).
  • The account view shows no second £651 debit to Co-Clean (event-001).
  • Supported only by documents the customer supplied:
  • The 15 Aug payment by Oakfield PM, made from the agency's landlord funds and not from her account with us (agency statement extract).
  • Co-Clean's written agreement to refund (forwarded email).
  • The bank has not verified either document. The plan treats them as the customer's evidence, and the reply describes the agency payment as "according to the agency statement you sent".
  • Small difference in wording: the event-001 note says she "spoke to" Co-Clean on 17 Sep, while the attachment is an email dated 17 Sep. Both say the same thing: Co-Clean agreed to refund, with no date given.
  • Gaps the plan records:
  • The evidence doesn't show the date of Co-Clean's next payment run, or whether the refund has been sent.
  • No bank recall procedure or eligibility rules were supplied. So the reply doesn't refuse a recall on policy grounds. It only explains how a recall works and why it is unlikely to be faster here.

3. Why this plan

Step s1: reply to the customer (the only step).

  • Explanation:
  • The confirmation shows a completed payment that the beneficiary bank received, and the account view shows a single debit.
  • The second payment came from the agency, according to the customer's statement extract.
  • Co-Clean has acknowledged the double payment in writing and agreed to refund £651, with no date.
  • So the evidence already answers her question. A recall is only a request that depends on the recipient agreeing, and Co-Clean has already agreed.
  • Evidence cited: event-001, event-002, event-004, payment_confirmation_2026-08-12.pdf, agency_statement_extract.txt, coclean_refund_agreement.eml.
  • Decision rules applied:
  • State only the payment stage the record proves.
  • A recall request is not a completed return.
  • Don't promise arrival times or outcomes without a commitment that applies.
  • Similar payments are not automatically duplicates on the same account.
  • Where the rule comes from: skill:payments. This is a handling rule from the payments skill, not a supplied bank policy and not a cited legal obligation. The reply therefore gives no policy or legal reason. It explains the mechanics: a recall asks the receiving bank to contact the recipient, and the money comes back only if the recipient agrees.
  • How this answers her question (event-004): from her side this was a duplicate. On her account, though, there is a single payment. The duplication arose because the agency paid separately. The recipient has already agreed to refund, which is the outcome a recall would be asking for anyway.
  • Practical suggestions in the reply:
  • Ask Co-Clean for the date of their next payment run and confirm her refund account details.
  • Tell Oakfield PM so the agency doesn't chase the same refund.
  • These are suggestions to the customer. They are not bank operations.
  • Authorisation: identity/authority is marked "not_needed". The reply gives information only and changes nothing on the account. The plan raises no safety concerns and sets no escalation triggers.

4. Execution boundaries

  • The only proposed operation is s1: sending the chat reply above to Linda Ferris. It has no prerequisites and no conditions, and it has not been sent.
  • The plan does not raise a recall, contact Co-Clean's bank or Oakfield PM, or change anything on the account.
  • The plan stops at this explanation. It does not cover what happens if the customer replies or still insists on a recall. That would need a fresh decision, and this memo does not propose one.

5. Expected result and limits

  • What s1 could achieve: Linda would get a clear explanation of:
  • the status of her payment,
  • why only one of the two payments is on her account with us,
  • what a recall can and can't do,
  • why waiting for Co-Clean's agreed refund, and chasing them for a date, is likely the quicker route.

    • If the reply is sent, the expected disposition is completed.

  • Still unconfirmed:
  • whether or when Co-Clean will send the refund,
  • the date of their next payment run,
  • that the agency statement and the Co-Clean email are genuine (the customer supplied both).
  • The reply must state:
  • The payment completed and the beneficiary bank received it.
  • Her account shows only one £651 payment to Co-Clean. The second was Oakfield PM's 15 Aug payment, according to her statement.
  • A recall is a request, not a reversal, and depends on the recipient agreeing.
  • Co-Clean has already agreed in writing, so a recall is unlikely to be faster.
  • We can't confirm the refund has been sent or when it will arrive.
  • The reply must not:
  • Say the bank can reverse the payment by itself.
  • Promise a recall would succeed or be faster.
  • Say or imply the refund has been sent or has a date.
  • Call this fraud, a scam or a bank error.
  • Give an invented policy as the reason.
  • Describe the agency's payment as a debit from her account with us.

ANSWER.md

Returned rent payment and new payee details: Sara Ahmed (case 020)

Decision

The plan is to reply in the in-app chat using only Sara Ahmed's own records. The reply would cover two things:

  • Her £950.00 standing order was returned because the old account is closed, and the money is back in her account.
  • The Confirmation of Payee (CoP) check on the new details was a Match. That does not prove the email came from the letting agency.

The bank would decline to say who holds or controls the new account, because that is another customer's confidential information. She would be advised to confirm the new details with Northgate Lettings by phone, on the number from her tenancy agreement, before she pays or changes anything. No change to her account, standing order or payments is proposed.

Expected disposition: completed (once the reply below has been sent).

Proposed actions (pending, in order)

  1. s1: Decline the third-party part of the question (pending). Do not tell her whether the new account is genuine, who holds it, or whether Northgate Lettings has any relationship with Harrow & Vale. The reason to give: the bank cannot share another customer's account or relationship information. This limit covers only that third-party information. Keep answering her questions about her own returned payment and the CoP result.
  2. s2: Continue the agent's chat reply (pending; depends on s1). Send the message below to Sara Ahmed in the in-app chat. It continues the agent's opening line in event-003.

Proposed customer message (not yet sent)

> Here's what I can confirm from your account. Your £950.00 standing order to Northgate Lettings Client A/C, reference RENT 9 LIME GROVE, went out on 24 Sep. The receiving bank returned it on 25 Sep with the reason "beneficiary account closed", and the £950.00 is back in your account. > > On the new details in the email, the Confirmation of Payee check came back as a Match. That means the name "HARROW & VALE RE NORTHGATE LET CA" matches the name held on that account. It doesn't tell us who sent the email, and it doesn't prove the change is genuine. > > I'm not able to share information about other customers' accounts, so I can't confirm who holds or controls that account. > > Emails changing bank details are a common route for scams. Please call Northgate Lettings on the number on your tenancy agreement, or another number you already trust (not one from the email). Ask them to confirm the new sort code, account number and account name before you pay or change anything. > > Your current standing order still points to the old account, which shows as closed, so it will need updating once you've confirmed the new details. You may also want to tell the agency your rent was returned so they know why it's late. > > We'll never ask you for your PIN, password or one-time codes.

Not proposed

  • No change to the standing order and no new payment.
  • No fraud escalation or payment hold. The evidence shows no payment to the new details.
  • No promise that the rent will arrive on time or that any loss would be refunded.

REASONING.md

Decision memo: case 020, returned rent payment and a "change of bank details" email

The full input is in the saved context.md. Event IDs and attachment names below refer to it.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records:

  • Returned payment (attachments/returned_payment_notice.txt; event-001):
  • A standing order of £950.00 to NORTHGATE LETTINGS CLIENT A/C (SYN-SC-20-2 / SYN-ACCT-20-2), reference RENT 9 LIME GROVE, was sent on 24 Sep 2026 at 06:00.
  • The beneficiary bank returned it on 25 Sep 2026 at 07:12 with reason AC03 – beneficiary account closed.
  • £950.00 was credited back to her account.
  • CoP check (attachments/cop_result_new_details.json; event-001):
  • The check ran on 2026-09-25T09:20:11Z against sort code SYN-SC-20-1, account SYN-ACCT-20-1, business account.
  • The name supplied was "HARROW & VALE RE NORTHGATE LET CA". The result was matched: true.
  • Internal note (event-001): this concerns the payee side of the new details. It is explicitly marked confidential to the third party's relationship with the bank.

Customer-supplied material:

  • Forwarded email (attachments/agency_email_new_details.eml):
  • Dated Fri 18 Sep 2026 and sent from accounts@northgate-lettings.example.
  • Says the client money account is moving to Harrow & Vale from 21 Sep 2026 and that the old account closes on 20 Sep.
  • Asks her to update her standing order to the new details.
  • Tells her to call the office "on the number on your tenancy agreement" with any questions.
  • Her chat message (event-002, also in request.md):
  • She suspects a scam because the account name includes the bank's own name.
  • She asks "Is this legit?" and is worried about losing £950 and being late with rent.
  • Agent's reply so far (event-003): "Good instinct to check before paying. Let me explain what I can and can't confirm."

Identity and authority are recorded as established for the in-app personal banking chat.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Returned payment: the notice and the account view match on amount, dates, payee and return reason. This can be stated to her as fact.
  • CoP Match: this shows only that the supplied name matches the account name held by the receiving bank. It says nothing about who wrote the email. A "change of details" email is the classic pattern of invoice or payment-redirection fraud, whether or not the details match.
  • Does the email look consistent? The closure date in the email (20 Sep) fits the AC03 return of the 24 Sep payment. That fit is not proof the email is authentic. The plan rightly does not rely on it either way.
  • Gaps the plan records:
  • *Has Northgate Lettings confirmed the change?* Only the customer can find out, by calling the agency on the number from her tenancy agreement. The bank cannot confirm this to her.
  • *Has she already changed her standing order or paid the new details?* Nothing in the evidence shows that she has.
  • Further gap: the context does not say who ran the CoP check at 09:20:11Z. The plan's wording ("the name she entered") assumes it was part of her own payee set-up. This does not affect the advice.
  • What cannot be established: whether the email is genuine or a scam. The plan therefore forbids saying either.

3. Why this plan

s1: Decline the third-party part (pending).

  • *Explanation:* the account view marks the payee-side fact as confidential to the third party's relationship. Confirming or hinting at it in answer to "is this legit?" would disclose it.
  • *Evidence:* event-001, event-002.
  • *Criterion:* limit disclosure to the requesting customer's own information and established authority, and do not disclose another party's private information.
  • *Source:* skill handling rule skill:privacy-documents. This is a handling rule from a skill, not a supplied bank policy document or a cited legal clause.
  • *Scope:* the decline is narrow. It covers only who holds the account and whether it is genuine. Her own payment and CoP questions are still answered.
  • *Order:* s1 comes first so that the reply in s2 is written within this limit.

s2: Chat reply (pending; requires s1).

  • *Explanation:*
  • The return notice and CoP result are her own records and can be stated directly.
  • A CoP Match confirms the name matches the account, not that the email came from the agency.
  • Checking independently through the tenancy-agreement number answers her concern without disclosing confidential third-party facts.
  • No payment to the new details is recorded, so a fraud escalation or payment hold is not needed now.
  • *Evidence:* returned_payment_notice.txt, cop_result_new_details.json, agency_email_new_details.eml, event-002, event-003.
  • *Criterion:* state only the stage the record proves; treat a request to change payment details as a warning sign; verify through a trusted contact route, not one supplied in the suspicious message; never ask for codes.
  • *Source:* skill handling rule skill:fraud-takeover. Again, this is a skill rule, not a supplied policy or legal obligation.
  • *Trusted contact route:* the tenancy agreement is a source she already holds, independent of the email. The email itself also points her to that number.

Alternatives the plan does not take:

  • *Changing or setting up the standing order for her:* excluded, because the new details are not yet verified and the plan forbids any account change.
  • *Fraud escalation:* excluded, because no payment to the new details is recorded.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Nothing has been executed. Both steps are proposed.
  • s2 depends on s1. The reply must respect the confidentiality limit.
  • The plan has no conditional branches and no escalation triggers (escalate_if is empty).
  • The plan stops at the chat reply. Updating the standing order is left to the customer, after she has verified the details with the agency.
  • Any new information would need a fresh decision rather than an extension of this plan. That includes her saying she has already paid or changed the standing order, or the agency denying the change.

5. Expected result and limits

What the reply could achieve once sent:

  • She knows her £950.00 is back in her account.
  • She understands what the CoP Match does and does not prove.
  • She knows why the bank cannot comment on who holds the account.
  • She has a safe way to verify the new details before paying.

What remains unconfirmed:

  • Whether the change is genuine.
  • Whether the rent will reach the agency on time.

The reply must state:

  • The 24 Sep £950.00 standing order was returned on 25 Sep with reason "beneficiary account closed", and £950.00 has been credited back to her account.
  • The CoP Match means the name matches the account name. It does not prove the email came from her letting agency or that the change is genuine.
  • The bank cannot share another customer's account or relationship information, so it cannot confirm who holds or controls the new account.
  • She should call the agency on the tenancy-agreement number, or another source she already trusts (not the email), and confirm the new details before paying or changing anything.
  • Her current standing order still points to the old account, which the return notice says is closed. It should be updated only after she has verified the new details.
  • She may want to tell the agency the rent was returned, so they know it may arrive late.

The reply must not:

  • Reveal or hint at the third party's banking relationship, or who owns the new account, or what type of account it is.
  • Call the email or the account genuine or safe, or say it is definitely a scam.
  • Present the CoP Match as proof that the change is genuine.
  • Change or set up the standing order or any payment.
  • Promise that the rent will arrive on time or that any loss would be refunded.
  • Ask for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

Plan

Intent: The customer wants to know whether the 18 Sep email from Northgate Lettings, which gives new client-account details, is genuine. Her £950 rent standing order was returned because the old beneficiary account is closed, and she wants to pay rent safely and on time.
Identity/authority: established

This applies only to that third-party information; keep answering her questions about her own returned payment and the CoP result.

You may also want to tell the agency your rent was returned so they know why it's late. We'll never ask you for your PIN, password or one-time codes."

Independent verification through the tenancy-agreement number answers her concern without disclosing confidential third-party facts. No payment to the new details is recorded, so no fraud escalation or payment hold is needed now.",

ANSWER.md

Case 021: Salary payment P-A after a connection timeout

Decision

Put the requested second GBP 2,400 payment (SAL-021-B) on hold and pass the case to Payment operations. Expected outcome: escalated.

Our records do not show that payment P-A (GBP 2,400, reference SAL-021-A, to SYN-ACCT-EMP021) failed:

  • The gateway accepted it at 09:00:01.
  • The only timeout was a status lookup at 09:00:05.
  • GBP 2,400 is still reserved against P-A.
  • Reconciliation is Pending, and no final execution status has been recorded.

If we send a second payment now, the employee could be paid twice. The case desk also has enquiry access only.

Proposed actions (in order, none carried out yet)

  1. s1: Hold the second payment. Do not submit or arrange the GBP 2,400 payment with reference SAL-021-B. Keep the hold until Payment operations reports P-A's final status.
  2. s2: Ask Payment operations to check the record. Ask them to review the execution record for payment ID P-A and send its final status (executed/sent, rejected or failed) back to the case desk. Include these details:
  3. Account SYN-ACCT-B021, Pennick Loomworks, sole trader Dara Pennick (authenticated)
  4. Payment P-A, client reference SAL-021-A, GBP 2,400.00, destination SYN-ACCT-EMP021
  5. The gateway accepted the submission on 2026-11-09 at 09:00:01. The status lookup timed out at 09:00:05. terminal_execution_status is null.
  6. The ledger at 09:06 shows a GBP 2,400 reservation under P-A, and reconciliation is Pending.
  7. The customer asked for a resend under SAL-021-B. It is on hold to avoid a duplicate. The customer says the salary is needed today.
  8. s3: Reply to the customer. Send this only after s1 and s2. The wording below assumes s2 has not been acknowledged yet.

Message to the customer (draft, not sent)

> Thank you for sending the exact message. It means the app couldn't get the payment status, not that the payment failed. Our records show salary payment P-A (GBP 2,400, reference SAL-021-A) was accepted when submitted at 09:00. GBP 2,400 is currently reserved on your account against it, and it is still pending, with no final result recorded yet. Because of that, we are not sending a second GBP 2,400 under SAL-021-B now. If the original payment also goes through, your employee would be paid twice. We are asking our Payment operations team to check the execution record for P-A and confirm its final status. That result is still pending. We'll update you once they confirm it. We can't confirm yet whether or when the funds will reach your employee.

*Use the "We have asked…" version only after Payment operations acknowledges the s2 request. That version is held separately as a future message.*

REASONING.md

Decision memo: Case 021, salary payment P-A after a connection timeout

The full input is saved in context.md. All the plan's operations are proposed and pending. Nothing has been submitted, held, requested or sent yet.

1. Situation and history

All times are on 2026-11-09, +00:00.

Authenticated bank records

  • E01 (09:00): The identity/session check passed. Dara Pennick approved GBP 2,400 salary instruction P-A, client reference SAL-021-A, to destination SYN-ACCT-EMP021. The same record says the case desk has *enquiry access only* and that Payment operations keeps execution records indexed by payment ID. request.md also says case-desk access is read-only.
  • E02 (09:00:05): The gateway packet attachments/gateway-log.json shows two entries:
  • a submission with status accepted at 09:00:01
  • a status_lookup with status timeout at 09:00:05
  • terminal_execution_status is null.
  • E04 (09:06): The account snapshot attachments/balance-view.csv for SYN-ACCT-B021 shows:
  • ledger balance GBP 5,000.00
  • reservation P-A of GBP 2,400.00
  • available balance GBP 2,600.00
  • reconciliation state for P-A: Pending

Customer statements (authenticated chat)

  • E03 (09:05): The customer quotes the app message: "Connection timed out while retrieving payment status." They say the employee needs the salary today and ask whether the payment failed.
  • E05 (09:07): The customer asks us to "send £2,400 again now" to the same destination, under the new reference SAL-021-B.

The deadline ("needed today") is the customer's statement. No record confirms it.

2. Evidence and gaps

What the records show

  • The submission was accepted (gateway-log.json, E02).
  • The only failure-like event is a timeout on the status lookup, not on the payment itself. The app message the customer quoted says the same thing (E03).
  • Funds are still reserved and reconciliation is Pending (balance-view.csv, E04). This fits a payment that is still being processed, not one that was rejected.

What cannot be established

  • P-A's final execution status (executed/sent, rejected or failed). This sits in the Payment operations execution record, which the case desk cannot see.
  • Whether the funds have been credited to SYN-ACCT-EMP021. There is no record either way.

Contradictions

  • None. The customer's concern comes from reading a status-lookup timeout as a payment failure, and the records do not support that reading.

3. Why this plan

All three handling rules below come from skill:payments, which is a skill's handling rule. No bank policy document or legal obligation was supplied, and none is relied on here.

s1: Hold SAL-021-B

  • Why: The timeout happened while looking up the status, after the submission had been accepted. It is not a recorded failure. The open reservation and Pending reconciliation show P-A may still execute. A new reference would not stop a duplicate payment to the same destination.
  • Evidence: gateway-log.json, balance-view.csv, history.md.
  • Rule: Only state the payment stage the record proves. A status timeout is not a failure. A second similar transfer must not be created while the first has no final status.
  • The desk's enquiry-only access (E01) separately means it cannot submit the payment.
  • The GBP 2,600 available balance does not change this. The reason for the hold is duplicate risk, not lack of funds.

s2: Ask Payment operations

  • Why: P-A's final status is the gap that decides the case. Payment operations holds execution records indexed by payment ID, and the case desk cannot see them.
  • Evidence: history.md (E01), gateway-log.json, balance-view.csv.
  • Rule: Request a named record lookup from the responsible team only where there is a material status gap. That condition is met here.

s3: Reply to the customer

  • Why: The customer asked whether the payment failed and wants a resend. The reply gives the recorded stage, says what it does not confirm and explains the hold.
  • Evidence: history.md, gateway-log.json, balance-view.csv.
  • Rule: Reply with the recorded status and what it does not confirm. Do not promise arrival times without a commitment that applies.

Why this order: The hold (s1) and the request to Payment operations (s2) come before the reply (s3 requires s1 and s2). This way the message describes the actual state of handling.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Dependencies: s1 and s2 are independent of each other. s3 depends on both.
  • Message wording: The draft in ANSWER.md says the Payment operations request is "being" raised. The "we have asked" wording may be used only once s2 is acknowledged, as the plan requires. That version is held as a future message.
  • Where the plan stops: It stops once the hold is in place and the request is waiting for Payment operations. The hold stays until they report P-A's final status.
  • What the plan does not cover: It does not decide what happens after that status is known. That needs a fresh decision. The plan has no escalate_if conditions, and no further branches are added here.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps can achieve

  • Prevent a possible duplicate GBP 2,400 salary payment.
  • Get the record that settles the question routed to the team that holds it.
  • Give the customer an accurate account of where the payment stands.

What they cannot achieve

  • Confirm whether P-A will execute or when the funds will arrive. Both stay unconfirmed until Payment operations reports back.

What the customer message must say

  • The records do not show that P-A failed. The gateway accepted the submission, and the timeout was only on the status lookup.
  • GBP 2,400 is reserved against P-A and reconciliation is Pending. No final execution status has been recorded yet.
  • We are not sending a second GBP 2,400 under SAL-021-B now, because the employee could be paid twice.
  • We have asked Payment operations to confirm the final status, and that result is pending. Before s2 is acknowledged, this becomes "we are asking".

What the customer message must not do

  • Say P-A failed, was rejected or was cancelled.
  • Say P-A was sent, settled or credited.
  • Submit or approve SAL-021-B, or say it has been submitted.
  • Promise arrival today or give any arrival time.
  • Say Payment operations has already completed its check.
  • Ask for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

Plan

Intent: The authenticated sole trader of Pennick Loomworks wants to know if the GBP 2,400 salary payment P-A (SAL-021-A) failed after an app timeout, and has asked us to send GBP 2,400 again to the same payee under the new reference SAL-021-B.

REASONING.md

1. Situation and history
